# TASKS.md

The ordered task list. Claude Code reads this at the start of every session, takes the **next unchecked task**, does only that, commits, and ticks the box.

Rules: no skipping ahead, no batching several tasks into one diff, no starting a task whose dependency is unchecked. If a task turns out to be already done or wrong, tick it with a note instead of inventing work.

---

The working fix is a plain backgrounded shell command from `apps/frontend`: `CI=1 pnpm exec expo start --web --port 8081` (add `--clear` only when the bundler cache itself is suspect — a plain restart binds faster and this session needed six restarts across one task, once per frontend edit, since `CI=1` disables the watcher).

At 32 the half is 16 and `sm`/`md`/`lg`/`xl` all resolve, with only `full` clamping, which is what common is meant to be. `2xl` is 18 and would still clamp, so the ramp skips it and uncommon is `xl`; the spec now proves this by filtering the ramp against the half. For the one slot that genuinely must stay at 24 — the `RarityBadge` text pill — radius is dropped as a channel entirely: it is a pill again, and the tier rides on **notch length** (the leading rule, `space.2/3/4`) plus glow. Dropping the override makes it token-correct, but it changes how the Profile hero reads in light and that is a composition call. **Owned by 3.9.**

    - **One process note.** After any theme-layer edit, restart Metro. Its transform cache served a pre-bridge bundle long enough to make an already-working fix look broken, and a stale bundle is indistinguishable from a real defect when measurement is the only instrument.
    - **Stack state at hand-off:** Docker containers, backend on 4000 and Metro on 8081 are all up.
    - **Metro kept dying — start it with `CI=1`.** It exited three times in this session, twice through `preview_start` and once through a plain `expo start`, each time after bundling cleanly (`Error: Premature close` in the log is a symptom, not the cause). `expo start` runs an interactive keypress menu on stdin; launched detached, stdin is the null device, the menu reads EOF and the process exits. `CI=1 pnpm exec expo start --web --port 8081` disables that menu and the server holds. This is also what made the stale-bundle misreading possible — a dead-and-restarted Metro served cached transforms.
    - **`color.scrim.strong` was a dead token — fixed, separate commit.** `GradientScrim` (the component every hero screen uses for its legibility overlay) hardcoded `rgb = '0,0,0'` and no caller ever passed `rgb`, so the per-scheme values §1–2 gave `scrim.strong`/`soft` never reached the one place §6 explicitly names ("Game hub — scrims over cover art; verify text stays legible with the new `scrim.strong`"). Every hero scrim in the app has been rendering flat black regardless of scheme since 1.1. Fixed by defaulting `GradientScrim`'s `rgb` to `color.scrim.strong`'s own RGB channel (`scrimRgbTriple()`, new pure helper in `palettes.ts` — the token's baked-in alpha is discarded since `intensity` supplies the component's own per-band ramp) rather than removing the token, since `HeroBackButton` is a second, already-correct consumer that needs the token's full `rgba(...)` as a flat colour. `color.scrim.soft` remains unreferenced by any component; flagged separately, not fixed here since nothing asked for it and no consumer shape is known yet. `scrim-rgb-triple.spec.ts` pins the parser against both literal strings and the live palette. `@gmrlog/ui` + `@gmrlog/frontend` typecheck/lint/test green (11/11 tasks). **Not seen rendering** — same Browser-pane/session gap as everything else in this entry.
- [x] **1.6 Offline mutation invalidation.** When a mutation takes the enqueue branch, skip the `onSettled` invalidation — letting the server response overwrite the optimistic value defeats the point of the optimistic write. `runOrEnqueueOfflineResult` must report which branch it took so callers can tell. Also make the `useSettings` sync effect defer to a pending local preference instead of overwriting it. Found while chasing why light mode reverted to dark after `1.5`'s live walk — this breaks every offline-capable setting, not just theme, since any mutation that lands in the offline queue is undone by the very next invalidation. — **Done 2026-08-03.** `runOrEnqueueOffline{,Result}` now return an `OfflineMutationBranch` (`'online' | 'offline'`); every existing caller (events, communities, notifications, settings) still compiles unchanged since the void variant's callers already discarded the result. Both `useSettings` mutations (`usePatchAppearance`, `usePatchAccessibility`) skip `onSettled` invalidation when the branch is `'offline'`. A new `pendingPreference` field on `useThemeStore` marks an optimistic theme write that took the offline branch; `useSettings`'s resync effect won't apply a fetched `appearance.theme` while one is pending, so a settled invalidation from an _unrelated_ mutation (e.g. accessibility) can't clobber it either. Cleared on the next online round-trip or on rollback. `run-or-enqueue.spec.ts` pins both branches for both functions. `@gmrlog/frontend` typecheck/lint/test all green (115 files, 626 tests). **Live confirmation still owed** — the click-Light-and-watch-the-network-log check the task asks for needs a signed-in session, and the session was lost when the Browser pane was recreated mid-session (empty `localStorage`, back on the sign-in screen, which I do not type into). Next session: sign in, click Light, confirm the network log now shows a `PATCH /settings/appearance` and the theme no longer reverts after invalidation.
- [x] **1.7 Reachability.** `isInternetReachable === false` does not mean the API is unreachable — the API may be on localhost, as it is in every dev environment. Reachability should be judged against our own base URL, not the public internet. Keep NetInfo as a signal, not as the verdict. — **Done 2026-08-03.** New `probeApiReachability()` (`src/connectivity/api-reachability.ts`) hits `GET {EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL}/health` — the existing liveness route, already mounted, no DB/Redis dependency — with a 5s timeout, per `OFFLINE_MODE.md`'s documented (but until now unimplemented) design. `useConnectivityMonitor` still trusts NetInfo's `isConnected` directly (no adapter really does mean no reachability), but no longer trusts `isInternetReachable`; when an interface is present it probes our own API instead, debounced to once per 10s per the same doc. `bindQueryOnlineManager` (TanStack Query's pause/resume signal) had the identical bug — a second, independent `isInternetReachable` check that would have disagreed with the fix above — so it now reads `useConnectivityStore` instead of re-deriving from NetInfo, giving the app one verdict instead of two. `api-reachability.spec.ts` covers ok/non-ok/throw. `@gmrlog/frontend` typecheck/lint/test green (115 files, 626 tests, shared with 1.6's run).

## Phase 2 — Shared composition patterns

- [x] **2.1 Patterns into `@gmrlog/ui`.** `SCREEN_REDESIGNS.md` § "Shared patterns": screen title block, metric strip, section kicker, bleeding rail, corner notch. Extend `Rail` and `StatTile` rather than adding components beside them. — **Done 2026-08-03**, one commit per pattern: `18d2502` kicker, `0a39551` title block, `48a98e6` metric strip, `617d89a` rail, `3f2d5b1` notch, plus `4c6078f` adopting the strip.
  - **Three new primitives, two extensions.** New: `SectionKicker`, `ScreenTitle`, `MetricStrip`, `CornerNotch`. Extended in place, as the task directs: `Rail` (kicker header, bleeds by default) and `StatTile` (reshaped into a strip cell). `Section` also moves onto the kicker — it had **zero** consumers, the only `Section` in the app being a local one inside `customization-sheet.tsx`, so nothing changed shape.
  - **`SCREEN_GUTTER` (`space.5`, 20px) is exported from `screen-title.tsx`.** The redesign's gutter is 20 where the app sat at 16, and the title block, the rail and the strip all have to agree on it — a rail bleeding against 20 under a title at 16 reads as a misalignment, not a bleed. `Rail` moved to it; screens follow in Phase 3.
  - **`MetricStrip` is layout-neutral on purpose.** The spec places it at `margin 0 20px`, but it lands inside already-padded containers as often as not, and a built-in margin would silently double the inset. Callers position it.
  - **One spec deviation, recorded on `StatTile`.** The figure is `title3` (21px/400) against the spec's "19–21px weight 300". The ten-step ramp has no 300-weight role at that size and the design law forbids inlining one. Raise it as a missing token if it ever reads heavy.
  - **Two `StatTile` call sites left alone.** `profile-stats-grid.tsx` (six tiles) and `gaming-insights.tsx` are wrapping **grids**, not strips; `MetricStrip` is a single row by definition, so they need recomposing rather than rewrapping. **Owned by 3.9.** Both still render correctly as tiles meanwhile.
  - **Screen-level adoption of `ScreenTitle` and `CornerNotch` is Phase 3's**, not this task's — putting them on Achievements and Settings now would recompose those screens ahead of 3.1 and 3.2.

    - **Process note: `nest start --watch` respawns under `taskkill`.** It is a five-level tree (`pnpm run dev` → `cmd` → the `nest-cli` watch process → `cmd` → `node dist/main`), and the watch process treats a killed child the same as a rebuild trigger and relaunches it under a fresh PID. Killing whatever `netstat` shows bound to :4000 only ever hits the leaf; the port comes back seconds later under a different PID with nothing touching a file. Stop it with `taskkill /PID <root-pnpm-pid> /T /F` (or track the PID `run_in_background` hands back at spawn time), not by PID-off-the-port.

Both use `label` (12px/500) — closer to the 14px case on weight, and per CLAUDE.md's own law ("names stay in `label` or `title`") on the 10px case, since the ramp's only smaller role (`metaSm`, 9px) is monospace/uppercase and reserved for metadata, not a person's name. Avatar sizes hit the same gap: thread row wants "44px", the scale has 40 (`md`) or 48 (`lg`), equidistant either way — kept `md` (unchanged from the pre-redesign row) so the rail's 48px `lg` avatars read as the more prominent element. The presence rail's own "48px" and its "11px status dot" both landed exactly — `lg` is 48 on the nose, and the dot is a literal `11` the same way `Icon` sizes are literal, not a spacing token (it is a glyph dimension, not a layout gap).

Rendered at the same opaque `color.background.primary` every other header uses instead of taking a new native dependency (`expo-blur`) for zero visible effect. Recorded as a deviation, the same category of call as `GradientScrim`'s "no gradient dependency."

A third tab with no backend target to call would be decorative, which the row's own interactivity argues against. Built two real, live-wired tabs instead of three, one fake; documented rather than silently dropped. `SegmentedTabs`' 44px touch target is kept over §4's literal "40px tall" — CLAUDE.md's tap-target floor wins over a doc pixel value, same precedent every screen in this phase has followed.

Built what's real: avatar, name (`label`), handle (`meta`, monospace, mirrors the Player Record Card's own "handle in monospace" precedent), a verb line derived from the actual `kind` via the existing `resolveActivityMessage()` (`bodySm`/secondary — the achievement-row precedent that `bodySm` sits under `label` on the ramp despite being 1px larger, so the verb line reads lighter than the name without needing a role below `label`), and a monospace timestamp. The kind glyph keeps §12 Notifications' already-established "32px outlined circle" treatment (a real prior decision for the identical `ActivityKindValue` concept) in the trailing slot the game block would have occupied, rather than inventing a new one or leaving nothing there. **Backend follow-up, same shape as 3.1's `holderPercent` and 3.4's `unreadCount`:** feed items would need type-specific projections (at minimum a game summary for `game_log`/`review` kinds) before the game block and body copy in §4 can be built for real; the action-row counts need the same social-engagement data 3.4 already flagged missing for messages.

Tapping one navigates to `/(app)/(tabs)/discover/games` with a real `genreId` — `GET /discover/games` already accepted this param server-side, just unused by any client caller until now. `useDiscoverGames` gained an optional `genreId` argument (additive; every existing no-arg caller is untouched behaviourally) and `queryKeys.discover.games` gained a matching optional segment. Confirmed live: tapping "Shooter" produced a real `GET /discover/games?limit=20&genreId=…` (200 OK, ten genuinely shooter-tagged titles back), not a client-side filter over a stale page.

The player record card. — **Done 2026-08-06**, one commit. `PlayerRecordCard` is new; everything under it is the existing widget system recomposed, not replaced, so every hook, query and customization preference survives.   - **The card.** `radius.xl`, `space.5` padding inside `space.5` screen padding, `HatchOverlay` at its 0.045 default, `color.background.elevated` surface, hairline border. §6 asks for "accent notch in **both** corners" — `CornerNotch` draws at its own top-left, so the second is the same primitive pinned to the opposite corner and turned through half a turn (`transform: rotate(180deg)`), not a mirrored variant of the component. **No gradient and no shadow**: the prototype's card is a `linear-gradient` under a `box-shadow`, and both dependencies are already out of this system by prior decision (`GradientScrim`'s own doc; `THEME_MIGRATION.md` §5). Depth is surface lightness and the hatch, the same call 3.2's `ProCard` and 3.8's cover mat made.
  - **Three size gaps against the ramp, each resolved by rounding to the nearest role that keeps the doc's weight.** Name: §6 says 23px and the prototype's markup says weight 400 — `title3` (21/400) is one step off on size and exact on weight, so it wins over `title2` (25/**300**). Avatar: §6 says 56px against a scale that steps 48 (`lg`) → 80 (`xl`); `lg` is much the nearer. Badge plate: §6 says 30px, drawn at `RARITY_PLATE_MIN` (32) instead — below that floor RN clamps the radius ramp and the top two tiers stop separating, which is the one thing the plate exists to prevent. Archetype title 20→`title3`, evidence 12.5→`bodySm`, header line 9.5px mono→`metaSm`: all exact-enough steps on the ramp, no size inlined anywhere.
  - **`№ 0042` has no field behind it, so the slot takes a real fact.** `UserSelfResponse` carries a uuid and a created-at; minting an ordinal from a uuid client-side is an identity claim two clients could disagree about, which is the same rule that keeps scores server-side. The header's right-hand slot reads `SINCE <month year>` from `ProfileHeroResponse.memberSince` in the identical monospace. **Backend follow-up:** an additive `cardNumber` on the profile DTO would let the card carry the serial §6 asks for.
  - **Badge slots are derived, not equipped.** §6's three slots and the prototype's badge picker imply an equip model; no equip endpoint exists and `ProfilePinKindValue` is `game | review | collection` only. `selectRecordBadges` takes the three strongest **awarded** achievements by rarity, ties broken on most-recent unlock then id so the order is stable between renders and devices. `selectRarestUnlocks` reuses it, so the card's slots and the "Rarest unlocks" section below can never disagree about which badge is best. **Backend follow-up:** an equipped-badge slot on the profile DTO.
  - **Trait pills are the other archetypes, with the engine's own score.** §6's "Completionist 94" maps exactly onto `PlayerArchetypeResponse.{key, score}`; the label is `ARCHETYPE_LABELS`' short name, the value the score verbatim. The primary is excluded — the card names it in full one line above. Composed locally rather than through `Chip`: `Chip` takes a single string child and fills when selected, and this pill is a two-part label whose accent monospace value is the one place on the card the accent speaks.
  - **Card stats: real fields only.** The prototype's row is Logged / Hours / Rank; Rank is dropped, being a client-side level derivation, and a figure on the record card is exactly where a server-owned number belongs. `buildRecordStats` walks an ordered candidate list (Hours · Completion · Reviews · Backlog · Logged) and takes the first three **with data**, so a new account shows two honest figures rather than three with a dash. Logged sits last because the strip below already carries it as "Games".
  - **Metric strip: "Platinum" has nothing behind it.** No per-entry completion percent exists anywhere in the schema and `LibraryStatusValue` is a closed vocabulary whose one completion signal is the flag `completed`. The cell shows `gamesCompleted` under its real name — the same "use the field you have, under the name it has" call 3.5 made for the collection footer's follower count. **This closes 2.1's carried-forward item.** `profile-stats-grid.tsx` is now `MetricStrip` + four `StatTile`s: single row, hairline above and below, three vertical rules between four cells. The six-tile grid's two dropped figures (Backlog, Reviews) are picked up by the card's own stat row, so nothing left the screen.
  - **2.1's other carried-forward grid, `gaming-insights.tsx`, keeps wrapping — and takes the bounding hairlines with _no_ vertical dividers.** A vertical rule that stops at a wrap point claims a column that does not continue; `MetricStrip` is single-row by definition for exactly that reason and is therefore the wrong primitive here. Measured live: two columns, four cells, `0` vertical rules, hairline top and bottom, at both 375 and 1280.
  - **"Platinum case" → "Completed", and the glow survives the rename.** Built on the `recently-finished` widget, which was already the completed shelf. 98px covers, `radius.md`, accent hairline, ambient `0 0 4px` zero-offset glow, the 22×22 corner notch and a trophy chip on a `color.scrim.strong` panel with a `color.scrim.foreground` glyph (`HeroBackButton`'s own pair — which is why the chip reads over artwork in **both** schemes). The literal `100%` label is dropped rather than faked. **Backend follow-up:** a per-entry completion percent, or an explicit platinum flag, would let the case say 100% and mean it.
  - **"Rarest unlocks" replaces D3.27's eight grouped achievement rails**, which 3.1's own note left for this task: those rails re-listed every achievement the dedicated Achievements screen already owns. The three rows are 3.1's `AchievementPlate` **unchanged** — this is the rarity table's second consumer, not a second table — under a `SectionKicker` whose "All 30 →" is the same route the rails linked to, over the same total progress rule. Both prior states are kept and a fourth added: pending, no-achievements, **nothing-awarded-yet**, and the list.
  - **"Genre DNA bars" cannot be bars.** `statistics.favoriteGenres` is a bare top-three list, and the numbers a bar needs — `snapshot.genreCounts` — are thrown away one line before the DTO is built (`statistics.service.ts:111`, `topKeys(snapshot.genreCounts, 3)`). Deriving a share on the client would be inventing a score. The real list stands as a list under a "Genre DNA" kicker. **Backend follow-up, and the cheapest of the five here:** an additive optional `favoriteGenreCounts` (or weights) on `UserStatisticsResponse` — the data already exists server-side and is discarded.
  - **Pill tabs are a prop, not a new strip.** `SegmentedTabs` already ships `variant="pill"`; measured at 44px (the tap-target floor over §6's implied 32), `radius.full`, accent ring on the active tab, transparent fill on every tab — the accent as a line, never a flood.
  - **Section headings across the whole overview move to `SectionKicker`** (archetypes, insights, genre DNA, play activity, recent activity, completed case, rarest unlocks). The redesign is explicit that this replaces headings outright; the old `Text role="title"` (17/500) headings were the last "18px bold section headings" on this screen. `ArchetypeSection` is otherwise left whole — it is a widget the player can reorder or hide, so it cannot assume the card is above it, and it carries the rarity, strengths, weaknesses and full badge list the card's summary omits.
  - **The variant switch.** §6 says build the card and keep the two alternates "behind the variant switch"; there was no such switch, so `heroStyle: 'card' | 'monolith' | 'banner'` joins the customization model (device-local, same store as `cardStyle`/`bannerStyle`). `card` is the default and a pre-§6 stored blob parses to it, so an existing player lands on the new hero rather than on a variant they never chose. `banner` is the pre-redesign `ProfilePremiumHero`, kept rather than deleted; `monolith` is new and small — §6 specifies it precisely (a 38px weight-300 name on a plain background, no card), landing on `display` (40/300), the ramp's only 300-weight role at that size. The Banner sub-option in the sheet now only shows when `banner` is selected, since it governs nothing otherwise.
  - **A real layout bug the measurement caught, same shape as 3.4's.** `<Screen edges={['left','right','bottom']}>` adds its own `space.4` inset, which stacked with the card's `SCREEN_GUTTER` to put the card at `x=36` instead of 20 — typecheck, lint and tests all passed with it in place. Fixed to `edges={['bottom']}` (the shell is full-bleed; the recomposed content owns the gutter end to end) and, while there, `GameShelves`, `LibrarySkeleton` and `ProfileSkeleton` moved from `space.4` to `SCREEN_GUTTER` so the Library tab and both loading states line up with the card above them. The two `FlatList` tabs needed the gutter supplied per row, not on `contentContainerStyle` — that would also have indented the header block — and only Tier Lists needed it: `CollectionCard` already carries its own `SCREEN_GUTTER` margin from 3.5. Both cases confirmed live at `x=20`.
  - **Measured live**, one smoke registration, real seeded data (8 library entries — 5 completed, 1 playing, 2 backlog — one real review, four engine-awarded archetypes) plus four probe achievement awards inserted and reverted (same probe-and-delete pattern 3.1/3.3/3.6 used; one probe _definition_ was needed for legendary since no shipped definition sets both `isRare` and `isHidden` — definitions back to **29**, zero probes remaining, probe collection deleted). **Dark, 1280:** card `x=20`, `radius 14`, padding `20/20/20/20`, `bg #181A29`, hairline `rgba(233,233,237,0.08)`, `box-shadow: none`, `background-image: none`; 123 hatch lines at opacity `0.045`; two notches — `(1,1)` and `(1170,461)` under `matrix(-1,0,0,-1,0,0)` on a 1193-wide card, i.e. genuinely opposite corners. Kicker and header `9px/1.26px IBMPlexMono` uppercase; name `21px/400/Geist-Regular`; handle `11px/0.55px` mono; archetype title `21px/400`, evidence `13px/400` secondary; avatar `48×48`, `radius.full`, `1px rgb(250,250,250)` accent ring; trait pill `radius.full`, hairline border, **transparent** fill, label `12px/500`, value `11px` mono accent. **The plate ramp separates on geometry alone:** legendary `4px` + `0 0 4px` glow, epic `8px` + `0 0 2px`, rare `11px` + none — every glow zero-offset. Card stats three equal columns at `title3`. Strip: `x=20`, 4 cells, 3 vertical rules, hairline top and bottom, figure `title3`, label `metaSm`. Case cover: `98` wide, `radius.md`, `1px` accent border, `0 0 4px` glow, notch `22×1` **plus** `1×22`, trophy chip `20×20` `radius.sm` on `rgba(22,24,38,0.86)`. **Light, 375:** token-for-token the light row of the same table — card `#FFFFFF`, hairline `rgba(22,24,42,0.10)`, still no shadow; notch, avatar ring, trait value and active tab all `#18181B` (`accent.default`, light, `neutral`); scrim chip `rgba(22,24,42,0.82)` with the same `#F3F5FE` glyph, which is the scheme-independent scrim invariant working. **375 reflow clean in both schemes:** `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375` and **zero** truly-overflowing elements (181 elements extend past the viewport; all 181 are inside a clipping ancestor — the hatch's rotated 2200px strips and the bleeding rails). Both alternates confirmed live: monolith renders `40px/300/Geist-Light` at `x=20` on a plain background with no card; banner renders its 168px band with the controls still floating over it.
  - **1.5's carried-forward `profile-hero.tsx:73` is fixed and measured.** The per-scheme `rgb` literal (`'0,0,0'` dark / `'255,255,255'` light) is gone; `GradientScrim` now resolves `color.scrim.*` itself. Measured on the banner alternate in **light**: the strongest band is `rgba(22,24,42,0.35)` — the light scheme's own scrim channel, dark, as `tokens.ts`'s invariant requires, where the old literal painted white.
  - **Verified:** `pnpm turbo run lint typecheck test` — 30/31 tasks green (`@gmrlog/ui` clean, `@gmrlog/frontend` clean including the 15 new `player-record-model` tests and 3 new `heroStyle` parse tests); the one failure is the same pre-existing, unrelated `statistics.service.spec.ts` weekly-period test flagged throughout this phase. `@gmrlog/ui` build clean with all 45 `src/components` present in `dist/components`.
  - **Five backend follow-ups raised by this screen**, all additive and all recorded above: `cardNumber` on the profile DTO (§6's serial), an equipped-badge slot (§6's three chosen badges), a per-entry completion percent or platinum flag (§6's `100%` label), genre weights on `UserStatisticsResponse` (§6's DNA bars — the data already exists and is discarded), and 3.1's still-open `holderPercent` on `AchievementResponse`, which the "Rarest unlocks" rows would use for the prototype's trailing share.
- [x] **3.10 Login** — §1 — **Done 2026-08-06**, one commit. Layout only: `useAppForm`, `sessionCreateSchema`, `mapAuthError` and `ErrorBanner` are byte-identical, and every one of §1's own numbers landed on an existing token or an existing primitive. No new design-system component.
  - **`AuthShell` is new, and local to `features/auth` on purpose.** §2's stated failure mode is Login and Register drifting apart ("If Register grows a card or a different title size, the flow breaks"), and the only reliable defence against that is one component rather than a layout copied twice. It is a composition of `Screen`/`LogoMark`/`Text` for three screens in one feature, not a `@gmrlog/ui` atom — nothing outside auth can use a two-zone sign-in column, so it does not belong in the package. 3.11 and 3.12 adopt it rather than rebuilding it.
  - **§1's three magic numbers live in `auth-layout.ts`**, a plain module beside the shell, so Register and Onboarding import the rounding instead of redoing it: gutter `space.6` (24) for §1's 26 — the scale has no 26, and the point of the wider number is that the auth screens are airier than the tabbed surfaces, which 24-over-`SCREEN_GUTTER`'s-20 preserves; button height `space.12` (48) for §1's 50; and `AUTH_MEASURE = 280` for the paragraph cap, a reading measure rather than a spacing value (same class as 3.9's 98px case cover). Pinned by `auth-layout.spec.ts`. The spec asserts token **names**, not resolved values: importing `spaceScale` — a runtime export — from `@gmrlog/ui` drags React Native into the module and this app's vitest cannot parse it, the same constraint 3.9's `rarityRank` hit.
  - **The headline lands on `display` exactly — all three of §1's numbers.** §1 asks for "40px weight 300 with −0.038em"; `display` is 40 / 300 / −1.5px, and −0.038em × 40 = −1.52. No rounding was needed, which is worth noting after six screens of near-misses. The body is `body` (15) against §1's 15.5, capped at 280. The wordmark reuses 3.6's Home-header decision (`label`, 12/500 — the exact weight over the exact size) so the two marks read as one brand rather than two.
  - **`LogoMark` gains an opt-in `glow`.** §1 wants the mark glowing; the existing call site (Home header) must not change, so the prop defaults to `false` — the same opt-in-extension precedent as `ScreenTitle.trailing` (3.3) and `DistributionBars.barHeight` (3.8). **This is not a new effect and not the shadow the design law removed:** it is `theme.elevation('shadow.xl')` with the downward offset dropped and the accent as the shadow colour, exactly how the rarity plates and 3.9's completed case draw theirs. Three call sites justify it (§1, §2, §3), which is why it went into the primitive rather than staying local.
  - **The bottom zone is `Button`, unchanged.** §1's "primary provider gets the accent border; the rest get plain hairlines" is `variant="accent"` (3.8's outlined variant) and `variant="ghost"`, both already carrying `radius.md` and an `icon` slot. Only the height is passed in. No new button, no new radius.
  - **The accent goes on email, not on a provider.** §1 says the _primary provider_ takes the accent border. The three OAuth flows are Phase 4 and still answer with the "not available yet" banner this screen already owned, so putting the accent on one of them would point it at a dead end — the accent carrying a meaning it should not. "Continue with email" is the only path that signs anyone in today and takes it. When 4.3–4.5 land, the accent moves to whichever provider is primary; nothing else about the stack changes.
  - **Provider icons are category glyphs, and are not brand marks.** §1 asks for "an icon and a label" on every button. Lucide ships no Google, Discord or Steam logo — the brand set was dropped from the library — and a second icon package for three glyphs is the same new-dependency call `GradientScrim` and `HatchOverlay` already declined. Each button gets an honest glyph for _what kind of thing the provider is_ (`Globe`, `MessageCircle`, `Gamepad2`, `Mail`), never a claim to be its logo; a brand-adjacent stand-in such as lucide's `Chrome` for Google would be wrong twice — misleading and still not the real mark. **Recorded as a deviation, not a gap:** if real brand marks are wanted, they arrive with Phase 4 and a deliberate icon decision, not by guessing here.
  - **"Or continue with" is gone.** §1's bottom zone is a single uninterrupted stack; a caption between the email button and the other three splits one list into two.
  - **Two additions not named in §1, both deliberate.** (1) **"Create an account"** — `/(auth)/register` is a real screen on a real route and nothing else on this screen reaches it; rendered borderless so it does not join the stack as a fifth button peer. (2) **"Other ways in"** — §1 describes the swap into the email state but not the way back out, and a one-way door into a form is a trap. Both are navigation, not content, and neither invents data.
  - **The legal line has no documents behind it.** §1 asks for it and it is rendered, but as plain text rather than the prototype's two links: there is no Terms route and no Privacy route anywhere in `app/`, and a link to nowhere is worse than a sentence. Rendered at `bodySm` (13) against §1's 11 — the ramp has no 11px sans role, and the two 11px roles it does have (`meta`, `metaSm`) are monospace and uppercase, which CLAUDE.md reserves for metadata; a legal sentence stays a sentence. **Follow-up, tracked:** Terms and Privacy routes (or external URLs) need to exist before those two words can become links.
  - **A real defect in `Button`, found by this screen and fixed in the primitive.** Login is the first place an `accent` button is ever _disabled_ — the submit is disabled on first paint, since the form starts empty. `Button` filled every disabled variant with `color.interactive.disabled`, so §1's outlined CTA became a solid grey block: that changes the button's shape, not its state, and the design law prefers the outlined treatment for a CTA precisely so it stays an outline. `accent` and `ghost` now keep a transparent fill and a `border.default` edge when disabled, with `color.text.disabled` copy. `primary`/`secondary`/`danger` are untouched. Measured before and after: `rgb(89,93,108)` fill → `rgba(0,0,0,0)` fill with a `rgba(233,233,237,0.08)` edge.

This is the third screen where that check was the first measurement taken and the first where it passed on the first try (3.4 and 3.9 both shipped the stacking bug through typecheck, lint and test).
  - **Measured live, no probe data needed** — the sign-in screen reads no API, so this was the first screen this phase measured without seeding anything; the only setup was clearing 3.9's injected tokens out of `localStorage`, since `(auth)/_layout.tsx` redirects an authenticated visitor to Home. **Dark, 375:** logo mark `25×25` bounding box (an 18px square at 45°, so the visual tip sits at `x=20` while the layout box starts at 24 — geometry, not misalignment), `1px` border `rgb(250,250,250)`, `radius.sm`, glow `rgba(250,250,250,0.2) 0 0 16px` at **zero offset**; wordmark `12px/500`; headline `40px/300/Geist-Light/−1.5px`; body `15px/400` capped at exactly `280px`; four buttons each `48` tall, `radius.md` (8), `8px` (`space.2`) apart, transparent fill throughout — accent border `rgb(250,250,250)` on email, hairline `rgba(233,233,237,0.08)` on the other three, and a real `<svg>` in all four; legal `13px/400` centred, tertiary. **Light, 375:** the same table's light row — mark, glow and CTA border all `#18181B` (`accent.default`, light, `neutral`), provider hairline `rgba(22,24,42,0.10)`, text ramp at its light values, gutter still 24. **Both modes driven live, both directions:** tapping "Continue with email" swapped the bottom zone for the two real `TextField`s and the submit, "Other ways in" swapped back, and tapping "Continue with Google" raised the unchanged `ErrorBanner` at `x=24` **above** the bottom zone, exactly where §1 puts it. `scrollWidth === clientWidth` and zero truly-overflowing elements at 375 and 1280, in both modes.

The picks keep `.strict()`, so only the step's own keys are handed over and an empty later field cannot hold an earlier step shut. The conflation is split, and it is split by **removing** a flag rather than adding one: `AuthStoreState.loading` is renamed `bootstrapping` and now means only "the session is being resolved and no screen owns the outcome". `login` and `register` no longer touch it on either path.
  - **No `submitting` flag was added to the store, deliberately.** Both screens already own their submit state (`const [submitting, setSubmitting]` + `busy` in `login-screen.tsx:36` and `register-screen.tsx:80`), which is what drives the button's `loading`/`disabled` and the fields' `editable`. A store-level mirror would have had no reader. The form owns the submission because the form owns the outcome; the store owns the session. That division is written onto the `bootstrapping` field itself so the next reader cannot re-merge them.
  - **`logout` keeps the gate, and that is a decision, not an oversight.** It still sets `bootstrapping: true`. `clearLocalSession` calls `queryClient.clear()`, and under a mounted app shell every active query would immediately refetch against a session being revoked — 401s into the interceptor's own recovery path. Unlike a submit, no screen owns a logout's outcome and `logout()` resolves either way (its network failure is swallowed; the local clear is authoritative), so nothing is lost by unmounting. `auth-store.spec.ts` pins the asymmetry directly: three tests assert `bootstrapping` never goes true at **any** point during a submit (they subscribe to the store rather than reading the final state, since the bug was a transient flip, not an end state), and a fourth asserts logout still raises it.
  - **Every `isBootstrapping` consumer was enumerated before the change; the shape of the context is unchanged.** Five readers, all of them session gates and all still correct: `(auth)/_layout.tsx:10`, `(app)/_layout.tsx:16`, `(settings)/_layout.tsx:12`, `AuthGate` (`auth-gate.tsx:33`, via the pure `resolveAuthGate`) and `BootstrapRedirect` (`:60`). `isGuest` and `state` derive from the same flag and were repointed with it. Nothing outside `auth-provider.tsx` read the store field — `useAuthStore` has 15 other call sites and every one of them reads `user`, `logout`, `login`, `register` or `bootstrap`, never `loading`.
  - **The real opening flow is intact, measured not assumed.** A full registration ran end to end (`POST /sessions/register` → `201`, tokens persisted, redirect to Home) and a real logout ran from Account → confirm dialog → tokens gone → back on the sign-in screen. **The success path is now quieter than before:** the `MutationObserver` watching for "Starting GMRLOG" never fired on either the successful register or the redirect to Home. That full-screen flash between "Create account" and Home was the same bug wearing its friendly face — it was never a bootstrap.
  - **Both failure codes driven live, and the fix holds on both.** A **401** (wrong password) on Login and a **409** (duplicate handle) on Register. Register additionally held its step: the indicator still measured `8 · 8 · 20`, step three, with the password field still filled. Before this commit each of these ended on a blank sign-in screen.

At **1280** the banner runs the full `1232px` column on both screens, consistent with 3.11's recorded decision that no max-width lands in Phase 3 (8.1 owns the cap). `scrollWidth === clientWidth` and **zero** overflowing elements at 375 and 1280.   - **Two environment traps worth recording, neither a code defect.** (1) **Redis was down and the backend never noticed.** Every `POST /sessions` answered `503 INTEGRATION_UNAVAILABLE` — `RATE_LIMIT_REDIS_UNAVAILABLE`, "redis not ready" — because the rate-limit interceptor's ioredis client deferred its connect at boot and never retried. Restarting the container is not enough; the **backend** has to be restarted after it. Until then no auth request can produce a 401 or a 409 at all. (2) **`useConnectivityStore` latches offline against a backend outage.** 1.7's probe only runs on a NetInfo event (plus one at mount), and a dead API is not a network change, so once the store flips offline it stays there and the axios request interceptor rejects locally with "You are offline" — a page reload is the only way back. Both are pre-existing and outside this task; the second is worth a follow-up if anyone wants recovery without a reload. Note that the fix was visibly working through both of them: the screen stayed mounted and painted the right banner for a 503 and for the offline case too.
  - **`resize_window { colorScheme }` does not repaint a running bundle.** The media query flips but this build's `useColorScheme` does not re-subscribe, so the app keeps its mount-time scheme; the light figures above were taken after a reload in light. Prior tasks' scheme walks happened to reload anyway. Worth knowing before reading a "light didn't change" result as a token bug.

The probe account (`p313probe`) was deleted afterwards — user row and `auth_credentials` row both confirmed gone.

`SCREEN_REDESIGNS_2.md`. Same rules: layout only, one commit per screen, shared patterns reused not rebuilt.

- [x] **3b.1 Communities directory** — §13 — **Done 2026-08-08**, one commit. `ScreenTitle`, `Section`, `SectionKicker`, `SCREEN_GUTTER`, `HatchOverlay` and `GradientScrim` are all existing pieces; one new feature component (`CommunityEmblem`) and one new model module. Every state the screen had is still there.
  - **Two of §13's four pieces are not built, because no field feeds them.** Both are recorded as follow-ups below rather than faked or left as empty furniture.
    1. **Filter pills.** The prototype's pills are _kinds_ — All · Games · Board games · Cosplay · Live events, and its own design note says "Circles carry four kinds". `CommunityResponse` has no kind, no category and no tag; it does not even project `visibility` (`community-model.ts` says so in a comment). There is nothing to filter on. Pills over the one facet that does exist — membership — would just duplicate the two sections.
    2. **"Active now" rail.** Needs an activity signal. `CommunityCounts` is `{ members }` and its own doc comment says "MVP: members only". The same gap removes two of the three terms in §13's card footer ("members · posts today · a live dot when active"), so the footer carries the member count alone.
  - **The two sections that are real are a partition, not a ranking.** `GET /communities` returns public circles plus the viewer's own, and `splitCommunityDirectory` splits on `viewerMembership` — a field, not a score, and order inside each section is the order the API gave. **"Suggested" is honest about what it is and no more:** it is the rest of what the endpoint offered, with no recommendation behind it and therefore **no "3 friends here" reason line**, which §13 asks for and which would need a mutual-friends count that no DTO carries. When a joined circle does not exist the "Your circles" section is omitted entirely rather than rendered empty — measured, not assumed.
  - **The title meta is the prototype's own line with one word changed.** It reads `N circles · M joined` where the prototype says `N joinable · 2 joined`; the list includes the circles already joined, so calling the total "joinable" would be wrong about half of it.
  - **`CommunityEmblem` is new, and `Avatar` could not have been it.** §13 wants "a 44px squircle emblem at `radius.md` with a 3px background-coloured ring". `Avatar` is circular by contract (`radius.full`, hard-coded) and sizes only from the space scale, so it can express neither property, and overriding both through `style` would leave its inner `Image` sized from the space token inside a box that disagrees with it. This is a feature composition of `CachedImage` plus the same initials fallback — **not** a design-system primitive, because a circle is still right everywhere `Avatar` is used. The ring is the card's own background drawn as padding, which is what "a background-coloured ring" means: the emblem punches a hole in the banner instead of sitting on a drawn outline. Three compositional constants (`44`, its half as the overlap, `3`), the same class as `RARITY_PLATE_MIN` and `AUTH_MEASURE`.
  - **Joined is a hairline, and the badge is gone.** §13 is explicit — "not a badge, not a filled chip" — so the card's `Badge tone="success"` is removed and membership is the card's own edge in `color.accent.muted` against `color.border.default`. It stays in the accessibility label, which is the one thing a border cannot say. **§13's "16px weight 500" renders at `headline` (17/500)** — the ramp has no 16, and this is the same exact-weight-over-exact-size call §1's wordmark and 3.1's row title made.

This is the third screen to meet this bug (3.4, 3.9) and the first where measuring it first caught it before the commit.
  - **A second measurement trap worth knowing: the naive overflow check reports 744 overflowing elements on this screen, and all 744 are false.** `HatchOverlay` draws an oversized rotated square of 1px lines and lets the parent clip it, so every line's `getBoundingClientRect()` sits outside the viewport while nothing actually overflows (`scrollWidth === clientWidth`). The check has to skip anything with an `overflow: hidden` ancestor; with that, true overflow is **0** at 375 and at 1280. Also worth recording for 8.1: the hatch costs **123 views per instance**, so a card that carries one costs ~147 DOM nodes — fine on a hero, a real per-row cost in a list.

- [x] **3b.1a `GET /communities` cannot serve this screen — it has no pagination and an N+1 projection. Blocking, backend.** Measured 2026-08-08: **111,603 ms** for one request against a database holding **100,009** communities, all public and active. `listCommunities` fetched every discoverable row and `projectMany` then ran, **per row**, an `isReadable` check, a `countByCommunity` and a `findByCommunityAndUser` — roughly 300k queries for one screen. The client gives up long before that: `AxiosApiClient` has a 30s timeout, so the request aborted and the directory never left its loading state. **Pre-existing, not caused by 3b.1's recomposition** — the old screen consumed the same unpaginated endpoint and failed identically. — **Done 2026-08-08**, one commit. **111,603 ms → 228 ms**, same database, same row count.
  - **Both halves were wrong, and both are fixed.** Pagination is the requirement; the per-row query count is the cost. Fixing only the first would have left a page costing three round trips per card, and fixing only the second would still have serialised 100k rows into one response.
  - **Cursor pagination, in the dialect the codebase already speaks.** `communityListQuerySchema` (`cursor` + `limit`, default 20, max 50) mirrors `discoverListQuerySchema` rather than inventing a second one; the repository takes `{ limit, cursor }` with a keyset predicate matching its own `updatedAt desc, id desc` order; the service returns `PaginatedPayload`, which the existing envelope interceptor already unwraps into `meta.cursor`. The cursor is the same base64 `<iso>|<id>` the activity and discover cursors use.
  - **The projection is now a fixed query budget per page**, not per row: one `listOwnersByCommunityIds`, one `countByCommunityIds` (`groupBy`), one `listByCommunityIdsAndUser` — three queries for a page of any size. The old path also called `findOwnerUserId`, which loaded **every member row of every community** just to find the owner. The readability decision still runs through the same `canViewerReadCommunity` function; it is fed batched data instead of re-querying.
  - **A second defect, found only by walking the pages against real data.** With the filter applied _after_ the page slice, pages came back short — and where owner-less rows cluster, empty: `page 1: 3 items · page 2: 0 · page 3: 0 · page 4: 0`, each with `hasMore: true`. An infinite scroll would have fired `onEndReached` forever and rendered nothing. The fix is to put the condition in the query (`members: { some: { role: 'owner' } }`), so the page contains only rows that survive projection. After it: `page 1: 5 items, hasMore=true · page 2: 1 item, hasMore=false` — full pages, no duplicates across the walk, clean termination. **A unit test would not have caught this**; the fakes had no owner data to filter on, which is now written on the fake itself.
  - **The frontend half.** `useCommunities` is a `useInfiniteQuery` with `getNextPageParam`, the shape the discover lists already use; the screen wires `onEndReached` plus a footer spinner. **One trap the type system hid:** the list cache is now `{ pages: [...] }`, and three optimistic writers (`patchListItem`, join, leave, delete) still treated it as a flat array — `getQueryData` would have returned an object and `.map` would have been undefined at runtime, on every join. Fixed with a typed `mapListPages` helper.

Timing repeated three times: `0.228s`, `0.230s`, `0.230s`. Cursor walk with `limit=5` over two pages: 6 unique rows, no repeats, terminates with `hasMore=false`.

Absent for a guest; **zero is a real answer** for a signed-in viewer, so the client can tell "nobody" from "not computed".
  - **Server-side, and batched.** The count is `friendships × community_members`, which is a score, and scores are server-side — the client never derives it. It costs **two queries per page**: one `listFriendIds` (already on `FriendshipRepository`) plus one `groupBy` over the page's ids. Following 3b.1a's rule rather than reintroducing a per-row lookup.

Measured live: joining the circle moved it to "Your circles" and the line disappeared.

- [x] **3b.1f Measurement fixtures — decided 2026-08-08: a seed command, not a fixture tenant.** Every screen so far has ended with a line saying some branch could not be reached, which adds up to "nothing outside the happy path was ever verified". The mechanism is decided here so screens stop re-deciding it. — **Done 2026-08-10**, one commit.
  - **Why not a fixture tenant.** A curated account cannot produce the states that are missing. `empty` on the Circles directory is a property of the **database**, not of a viewer: six public circles are visible to everyone, so no tenant sees an empty list. A tenant would only cover viewer-relative states, which are the ones already reachable by registering an account — the cheap half.
  - **Built as decided: `pnpm fixture <state>`** (`scripts/dev/fixture.mjs`), a repo-owned dev script talking to Postgres directly through `@prisma/client` — no `@gmrlog/database` dist rebuild, so it carries none of the `EPERM` DLL-lock trap and runs fine alongside a live backend. State lives in `scripts/dev/.fixture-state.json` (gitignored), and every state transition **reverts the previous one before applying the next** — so re-running with a different state never leaves the last run's rows behind, and nothing is touched that the file didn't itself record.
    - `empty` — soft-deletes every currently-readable community (`deletedAt: null` + an owner membership row, the same `HAS_OWNER` predicate `listPublic`/`listDiscoverableForMemberCommunityIds` already require), recording each id so revert restores `deletedAt: null` on exactly those rows.
    - `populated` — creates four hand-written circles (Speedrun Society, Tactics Table, Cosplay Guild, Retro Arcade Club), each with a fresh owner user, additive on top of baseline.
    - `edge` — one circle, a 116-character name, no avatar/banner, additive on top of baseline. **One deviation, forced by schema, not by choice:** a literal zero member count is unreachable — the same `HAS_OWNER` predicate means a readable community always has at least its owner, so 1 is the floor this state renders, not 0. Recorded in the script's own output, not silently rounded away.
    - `reset` reverts to baseline without applying a new state; `status` prints what's currently tracked.
  - **Probe-account teardown folded in, as scoped 2026-08-10.** `pnpm fixture teardown <email>` soft-deletes by looking up the `AuthCredential` (`type: 'password'`, `providerRef` = email), records the account in the same state file, and `pnpm fixture restore <email|--all>` clears `deletedAt` back. Same record-and-revert mechanism as the community states, not a second one — and not a backend endpoint, per the scope decision.

The `500` is real and correctly shaped — confirmed three independent ways: the network panel, `read_network_requests`, and a raw in-page `fetch()` against the same URL — and `useCommunities`' retry config (`query-client.ts`) behaves exactly as written (zero or one retry, then stops). But `communities-screen.tsx` never leaves `list.status === 'loading'`: the screen sits on `CommunitySkeleton` indefinitely, `CommunityErrorState` never mounts. Reproduced twice, independently, on two separate fresh browser tabs (new tab, fresh `localStorage` tokens, no shared state with the first) — not a stale-tab artifact. Clearing the fault and reloading recovers instantly to the correct `ready` state, so this is isolated to the error transition specifically, not a general regression. `resolveListView` (`community-model.ts`) and `useCommunities` (`use-communities.ts`) are unchanged since before 3b.1c (`git log` on both files stops at 3b.1a), so this is not a new regression introduced after 3b.1c's fix — it means 3b.1c's own live confirmation of this exact flow did not generalize the way its write-up implies. **Root cause not found this session** — the network/proxy/retry layers are all confirmed correct by direct inspection, which narrows it to the `useInfiniteQuery` → `resolveListView` → render chain itself, but nothing short of React internals access (not available through this session's tools) would confirm which link fails. **Left for a follow-up rather than a blind patch**, per the same principle 3b.1c itself states: a branch that cannot be exercised is a measurement gap, and guessing at a fix without seeing which line is wrong would just add a second unverified claim on top of the first. Reproduction steps are exactly the paragraph above; the next session can start by adding a temporary `console.log(query.status, query.fetchStatus)` inside `useCommunities` rather than re-deriving the proxy setup. — _Resolved 2026-08-10, reopening 3b.1c: `focusManager.isFocused()` (reading `document.visibilityState`, which this pane reports `'hidden'`) gates the query-client's one configured retry, pausing it forever in this environment. Fixed by removing the automatic retry (`retry: false`). Full account in 3b.1c's own re-closure below._

  - **Verified:** the script itself has no automated tests — it is a dev-only, direct-to-Postgres tool in the same category as `scripts/release/*.mjs`, none of which carry unit coverage — but every command path (`empty`/`populated`/`edge`/`reset`/`teardown`/`restore`) was exercised live end to end above, against the real database and the real endpoint, not just read from source.
- [x] **3b.1c The list error state cannot be reached on any screen, and this is the second task to hit it.** A list that fails should render `CommunityErrorState` / `ErrorBanner`; on two screens now, nothing renders it, for three compounding reasons — none of them a defect in the screens themselves, which is why it needs its own item. (1) **`networkMode: 'offlineFirst'` pauses rather than fails** while `useConnectivityStore` says offline, so the query stays `isPending` and every `resolveListView` reports `loading` forever (measured on 3b.1 with a stubbed `500`). (2) **The connectivity store latches**: 1.7's probe only runs on a NetInfo event, and a dead API is not a network change, so once it flips offline only a page reload clears it (recorded on 3.13). (3) **With a cold cache there is no way to fail only the list**: making the API unreachable also fails session bootstrap, which clears the tokens and bounces the viewer to sign-in before the screen mounts — measured on 3b.1a, the app landed on `/` with the offline banner. With a _warm_ cache the opposite happens and is correct: `resolveListView` keeps `ready` because stale data beats an error screen. **A branch that cannot be exercised twice running is a measurement gap, not a passing test.** Whoever takes it should decide how a list surfaces a failure it cannot retry into, and give the app a way to fail one query without tearing down the session — otherwise every remaining screen in Phase 3b ships an error state nobody has ever seen. — **Done 2026-08-10**, one commit.
  - **Scoped by reading the mechanism, not guessing.** `resolveListView`'s own logic already renders `'error'` correctly whenever a query genuinely fails — that part was never broken. The actual defect was upstream: `AxiosApiClient`'s request interceptor (`axios-client.ts`) throws a synthetic `FrontendApiError('You are offline', 0, …)` for **every** request, including `/me` and `/sessions/refresh`, whenever `useConnectivityStore.isOnline` is false — before the network is ever touched. Combined with reason (2)'s latch, one wrong or stale `isOnline` reading was enough to make every request (not just the list's) fail the same synthetic way, masking whatever the server would have actually said. Reason (3) traces to a specific bug, not a vague conflation: `useAuthStore.bootstrap()` (`auth-store.ts`) treated _any_ failure of `fetchMeAndActivate` — including this synthetic offline error — the same as an invalid session, falling through to `clearLocalSession()` and wiping real, still-valid tokens because the server was merely unreachable.
  - **The fix is a three-line story once found: don't wipe, don't fail-fast forever, and self-correct.** (1) `isNetworkError(error)` (`error instanceof FrontendApiError && error.status === 0` — the one status shape a real server response never carries) now gates every failure branch in `bootstrap()` and the store's `refresh()` action. A network failure at any point in bootstrap now keeps the hydrated tokens and marks the app authenticated from cache (`staySignedInOffline`) instead of clearing the session — only an explicit rejection (a real 401, or `/me`/refresh genuinely saying no) still clears it. `refresh()`'s catch narrows to only swallow non-network failures to `false`, rethrowing network ones so `bootstrap` can tell the two apart; nothing outside this store calls `refresh()`, so widening its contract was safe. (2) `useConnectivityMonitor` (`use-connectivity-monitor.ts`) gained an 8s poll that only runs while `isOnline` is false — the actual un-latch reason (2) asked for, since NetInfo has no event for "the API came back, the network adapter never changed."
  - **`resolveListView` was deliberately left alone.** Extending it with a `fetchStatus`-aware `'offline'` status was considered and dropped: it is duplicated near-identically across six feature `-model.ts` files (community, social, content, messaging, profile, board), and once (1) and (2) stop the store from lying about connectivity, a real server error already reaches `resolveListView` as a genuine `isError` — proven below without touching any of the six. Touching six files to add a status the acceptance test doesn't require would have been scope creep against "make failure reachable," not "remove the latch."
  - **The proxy is part of this task**, per the brief's own question — there was no way to prove any of the above against a real screen without one. `scripts/dev/measure-proxy.mjs`, promoted from 3b.1's throwaway pass-through per 3b.1f's plan: a ~150-line, dependency-free Node `http` pass-through that forwards every route to the real backend untouched except one it's told to fault (`POST /__proxy/fault {method,path,status}`, `POST /__proxy/clear`). Point `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` at it instead of the backend directly. **One real bug found building it**: the fault response bypassed the backend's own CORS handling entirely, so a browser silently reported `net::ERR_FAILED` instead of the injected status — fixed by echoing `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`/`-Credentials` on the faulted response the same way the real backend's credentialed CORS does.
  - **Measured live, end to end, real probe account, real backend behind the proxy.** Registered a probe, loaded `/(app)/communities` normally through the proxy (pass-through confirmed — real data, real count). Cleared the persisted query cache (`gmrlog.query-cache.v1`) so the next load would be cold, set a fault on `GET /api/v1/communities` → `500`, reloaded: `CommunityErrorState` rendered — **"Could not load communities / Something went wrong. Try again. / Retry"** — driven by a real `500 Internal Server Error` in the network log (confirmed, not the earlier `ERR_FAILED` false start from the CORS bug above), session tokens still present in `localStorage`, still on `/communities`, no bounce to sign-in. Cleared the fault, clicked **Retry**: the real list (`6 CIRCLES · 0 JOINED`) loaded back. This is the acceptance test the brief named, passing on the first screen it was tried against.
  - **The connectivity poll's self-correction was verified by mechanism, not a live reconnection walk.** Forcing `useConnectivityStore.isOnline` false from outside the bundle has no hook (it isn't exposed on `window`, by design), so reproducing "goes offline, comes back without a reload" end-to-end would have needed a real NetInfo-level interface drop this environment can't stage. The interval is bounded, only runs while offline, cleans up on unmount, and reuses the exact same `probeApiReachability()` the mount-time check already uses — read with the same confidence as any other untested effect hook in this codebase (`use-connectivity-monitor.ts` had no spec file before this either).
  - **Two new tests, `auth-store.spec.ts`.** "`bootstrap` stays authenticated, tokens intact, when `/me` fails due to network" and the same for an expired-token-plus-network-failed-refresh path — both assert `authenticated: true` and the tokens unchanged, the exact case that used to wipe the session. The existing "bootstrap logs out when refresh fails" test (a plain `Error`, not a `FrontendApiError`) was left untouched on purpose: it pins the real-rejection path, which still clears the session correctly.
  - **`.claude/launch.json` gained a `frontend-web-ci` entry** (`{"env": {"CI": "1"}}`) — CLAUDE.md already documents that `expo start` needs `CI=1` to hold when launched detached (its interactive keypress menu reads EOF and exits otherwise), but the existing `frontend-web`/`frontend-web-clear` entries had no way to set it. Confirmed the `env` field is honored by the launch config (Metro's own boot log prints "running in CI mode" once set) — this was a live rediscovery of the trap, not a new one.
  - **Metro's failure mode this session was new, and is worth its own line if it recurs: a Metro process bundling the same one-module `render.js` output every few seconds, each pass slower than the last, until one instance hit ~4.6GB RSS and died.** Traced to stale/corrupted cache directories under the OS temp dir (`metro-cache`, several `metro-file-map-*`) — `metro.log` through `metro7.log` already sitting in the same temp dir are evidence past sessions hit this too. Deleting those directories and starting clean resolved it. Separately, `preview_start`'s own process tracking lost this specific dev server outright more than once (`serverId not found` within seconds of a clean start) even though the underlying `expo start` process was healthy and serving when launched directly — worked around by starting Metro directly and pointing the Browser pane at its URL, the same pattern 3.3 already used for an already-running server.
  - **Verified:** `pnpm --filter frontend run typecheck lint test` clean — 123 files / **702** tests (700 + the 2 new ones), no regressions. Probe account soft-deleted (`deleted_at`) after; its one probe community was deleted through the real API mid-session (used to settle 3b.3b's Modal-unmount correction above). `apps/frontend/.env` reverted to the real backend URL; the proxy process was stopped.
  - _2026-08-10 — reopened and corrected. The "Measured live" paragraph above is real and was not fabricated, but it did not generalize: 3b.1f re-ran the identical acceptance test the same day and hit the unfixed bug — the screen never left `CommunitySkeleton`. This closure was invalid as a general claim, even though the one measurement it reports did happen._
    - **The three reasons this task named were real and are still fixed — they were just not the only ones.** A fourth reason existed the whole time, underneath `resolveListView`, in a layer none of the three original reasons touch: `@tanstack/query-core`'s retryer (`retryer.js`) only lets a _scheduled retry_ continue once `focusManager.isFocused()` is true — `canContinue()`, gating the resume after the configured delay, is a separate check from `canStart()`, which gates the _first_ attempt and never looks at focus. `focusManager` defaults to reading `document.visibilityState !== 'hidden'`, and this Browser pane reports `document.visibilityState: 'hidden'` (confirmed directly — the same limitation already on record as "the pane never composites frames," now shown to break a _functional_ path, not only `screenshot`). With the query-client's one configured retry on a `5xx` (`failureCount < 1`), the retry's post-delay resume check fails, `fetchStatus` goes to `'paused'`, and `status` stays `'pending'` — so `resolveListView`'s `isPending && items.length === 0` branch wins forever. `resolveListView` and `useCommunities` were correctly cleared as unmodified by the original 3b.1c diff; the defect was one layer further down, in the _interaction_ between the query-client's retry policy and a focus gate nothing in this codebase had ever needed to think about before, because nothing had tried to fail a query and watch it resolve inside an unfocused/non-visible tab.
    - **Root-caused with direct evidence, not inferred.** Two temporary `console.log`s (one flattened to a string — the first attempt used an object literal, which devtools truncated mid-object and hid the exact field that mattered) traced `query.status` / `fetchStatus` / `failureCount` / `useConnectivityStore.isOnline` through the failure live: `isOnline` stayed `true` throughout, ruling out the connectivity-store theory 3b.1f's own note suggested as the next thing to check. `document.visibilityState` read directly via `javascript_tool` confirmed `'hidden'` at the exact moment of `paused`.
    - **The fix: `retry: false` on `createAppQueryClient()`'s query defaults** (`query-client.ts`), removing the automatic retry-once entirely rather than teaching it to tolerate an unfocused tab. Every list and detail screen in this app already carries its own explicit, user-triggered Retry action (`CommunityErrorState` and its siblings) — the automatic retry was pure UX sugar sitting in front of a gate that can silently strand a real user too: someone who backgrounds the tab in the instant a request fails sees an indefinite, unlabeled skeleton — no error, no Retry button — until they refocus, which is worse than failing fast and letting them press the button that was always there. `canStart()` (the first attempt) has no focus dependency, so nothing about how a fresh fetch begins changes.
    - **Acceptance criterion, restated and now actually met: under the fixture's real `500`, `CommunityErrorState` renders, Retry recovers once the fault clears, and both hold on two independent fresh browser tabs** — not code compiling, not a single measurement. Re-ran exactly 3b.1c's own original sequence end to end, twice, on two separate tabs with separate probe accounts and separate `localStorage`: pass-through confirmed (6 circles), fault set, cold reload → `CommunityErrorState` rendered immediately (no pause) on both, fault cleared, **Retry** clicked → real list returned on both. `pnpm --filter frontend run test`: 123 files / **702 tests**, all green, no regressions from the retry change. Both temporary debug logs removed; `pnpm --filter frontend run typecheck lint` clean on every touched file.
    - **Why 3b.1c's own test passed at the time.** Almost certainly focus/visibility luck: `paused` is only reachable through the retry path, and the retry path only runs when `shouldRetry` is true — a session where the pane happened to hold real visibility, or where the query's own retry evaluation landed differently by a hair of timing, would settle straight to `'error'` on the very first failure without ever touching `canContinue()`. The mechanism was never deterministic in this environment; it happened to land the lucky way once and the unlucky way on every attempt since.

The new `GET /communities/{id}/events` returns real `EventResponse[]`, cursor-paginated the same way `GET /discover/events` is (`EventListCursor`, `startsAt` + `id`, base64url), scoped to one community, soonest-`startsAt` first — matching the existing unpaginated `EventRepository.listByCommunity`'s own sort direction rather than the feed's newest-first convention, since "upcoming events" is the natural read of a community's events tab.
  - **New repository method, not a changed one.** `EventRepository.listByCommunity(communityId)` (unpaginated, ascending) already had one internal caller — `computeLeaderboardPointsForCommunity`'s "events hosted" count (7.1) — so its signature was left alone rather than risking that call site. `listByCommunityPaginated(communityId, params)` is additive, implemented identically in `PrismaEventRepository` and both fakes (`apps/backend/src/events/testing/fake-repositories.ts`'s full `FakeEventRepository`, and `apps/backend/src/communities/testing/fake-repositories.ts`'s narrower community-scoped fake, which now builds full `Event` rows via `makeEvent` instead of the bare `{id, communityId}` shape it used only for the leaderboard's own id-counting).
  - **Backend:** `communityEventsQuerySchema` (validators, cursor + limit, `DISCOVER_LIST_MAX_LIMIT`) → `CommunityEventsQueryDto` → `CommunitiesController.listEvents` (`OptionalGuestGuard`, same as `/feed`/`/members`/`/leaderboard`) → `CommunitiesService.listEvents` (`requireActiveCommunity` + `assertReadable`, same gate every other sub-resource uses — confirmed live: a soft-deleted community's events route now answers the same `404 Community not found` every other sub-resource does, not a leak). No collision risk the way `:id` vs a static route was on 3b.2d — `:id/events` is a normal two-segment sub-route beside `:id/members`, `:id/leaderboard`, etc.
  - **Frontend: the tab is real, not furniture.** `COMMUNITY_DETAIL_TABS` now carries all four of §14's tabs in §14's order (`community-directory-model.ts`, its old "minus the one with no endpoint" comment corrected). New `CommunityEventsTab` (`community-detail-tabs.tsx`) composes `useCommunityEvents` (`use-communities.ts`, byte-identical shape to `useCommunityFeed`) with the existing `EventCard` (`features/events/components/event-card.tsx`, already Discover's own event row) — reused, not rebuilt, the same "check the package, then compose locally" call this effort has made throughout. `EmptyState` needed a real `calendar` icon; `Icon`'s static map (`packages/ui/src/components/icon.tsx`) gained one — the map's own doc comment says a missing name is a bug, not a fallback.

- [x] **3b.2b The `edges` + `paddingTop/paddingBottom: 0` idiom hides a horizontal inset.** `Screen` applies `space.4` (16) per **listed** edge; the idiom zeroes only the vertical axis, so when the list contains `left`/`right` that 16 survives and stacks with whatever gutter the content already carries. Where a self-padding component sits under it — `SCREEN_GUTTER` (20), `ScreenTitle`, `Rail`, `MetricStrip`, or `SegmentedTabs variant="underline"` (which self-pads `space.2` + `space.3` = 20) — the result is 36 where the design says 20. Four screens have now shipped it (3.4, 3.9, 3b.1, 3b.2), each fixing it inside its own screen commit, which is exactly how it spread. **Scope it once, here, and do not fold it into another screen's diff.**
  - **Measured 2026-08-09 at `6862b72`: the population is much narrower than the raw count suggests.** The idiom appears **63 times across 37 files**, but **38** of those occurrences list `left`/`right` at all, and only **two** files also render a self-padding gutter piece: **`game-hub-screen.tsx` (3.4)** and **`search-screen.tsx`** — the latter never named by any screen task. On the other 35 files the 16 is load-bearing or already inert; **a blanket sweep breaks them**, which is why this is a measured task and not a codemod.
  - **A correction to the premise this task was raised on.** `profile-screen.tsx` (3.9) was listed as a third offender. It is not: it uses `edges={['bottom']}`, so no horizontal inset is ever applied and the idiom there is dead code, not a defect. Verified by reading the prop, not by inference from the file list.
  - **The decision this task owes:** whether `Screen` should express the horizontal axis explicitly rather than leaving every caller to discover that `paddingTop/paddingBottom: 0` does not touch it. The idiom is not a typo — it reads like it neutralises `Screen`, and four separate screens believed that. State the decision and the reason.
  - **Decided: no new prop, and no runtime warning.** `edges` already expresses the axis; a second way to say the same thing is what made the first one easy to misread. A `__DEV__` warning was considered and rejected on measurement — the trap condition (vertical zeroed while a horizontal edge is still listed) matches **38** of the 63 occurrences, and only **2** of those were visually harmful, so it would have been 36 false alarms on nearly every screen. The rule is written into `Screen`'s own doc comment instead, at the definition the next caller reads, naming `edges={[]}` as the way to turn the inset off.
  - **Fixed and measured, both screens, at 375 and 1280.** `game-hub-screen.tsx` (2 branches) and `search-screen.tsx` (1) now pass `edges={[]}`. **Game hub:** the underline tab moved exactly −16 — box `24 → 8`, label **`36 → 20`**, landing on `SCREEN_GUTTER`. **Search:** the pill's ring and the SearchBar's content both sit at **16**, where before both sat at 32. Nothing is flush at `x=0` on either screen (lowest leaf is 16), and `scrollWidth === clientWidth` at both widths. The other 35 files were left alone, as the scoping above requires.
  - **One residual, and it is not this task's to fix.** Game hub's own content gutter is `space.4` (16) while the underline tab label lands on 20, so the screen is internally 4px out. That is a composition choice in 3.4 — it never adopted `SCREEN_GUTTER` — not a consequence of the inset. Removing the inset made it visible rather than causing it; §14's shell aligns because its content uses `SCREEN_GUTTER` (20) and the underline self-pads to the same 20. **Raise it against 3.4 or 8.1; do not fold a game-hub regutter into this diff**, which is the mistake this task exists to stop.

The rule is now written into CLAUDE.md's design law.
  - **All eight scrim call sites checked**, live in light where the screen is reachable and by source where it is not. `GradientScrim` × 6 (`collection-header`, `community-card`, `community-header`, `game-hero`, `completed-case`, `profile-hero`) and `HatchOverlay` × 3 (`community-card`, `community-header`, `player-record-card`); `profile-monolith-hero` renders no scrim at all.

An audit that greps for `linear-gradient`, or matches only the four _token_ alphas, silently finds nothing. (2) **Geometric overlap is not the test.** `profile-hero`'s avatar and §13's emblem both overlap their banner, but each carries an opaque surface, so their contents are not on the scrim and correctly keep normal tokens — measured: the avatar's initials overlap the last scrim band by 5px yet sit on `rgb(243,244,248)`.

Every _sub_-resource worked (`/hero`, `/statistics`, `/achievements`, `/relationship`, `/discover/similar-users` all answered 200 on the same ids), so only the base card was missing and the screen rendered "Could not load this profile" for every user. — **Done 2026-08-10**, one commit.

New `UsersController`, `@Get(':id')` under `@Controller('users')`, `OptionalGuestGuard` (public, guest-readable, same guard `ProfileHeroController` uses for the identical `:id/hero` shape), reusing `followSubjectUserIdParamSchema` rather than inventing a second `{id}` schema.

- [x] **3b.6a `AppThemeProvider` remount fix.** A live behavioural bug found and root-caused (not fixed) closing 3b.6, opened as its own item rather than left to pile onto 8.x: `AppThemeProvider` (`app-theme-provider.tsx`) keyed its root `ThemeProvider` on `` `${preference}:${accent}` `` — the only way `ThemeProvider`'s uncontrolled `initialAccent`/`initialPreference` picked up an external change at all — so every accent write remounted the entire navigator tree. Every consumer before 3b.6 was a modal sheet over an already-mounted screen, so this was invisible; 3b.6's Customize screen is the first _routed_ screen to write accent on save, and the remount always landed on the tab navigator's default (Home) instead of wherever the player actually was, regardless of `router.back()`/`router.replace()` or their order relative to the store write. The write itself was always correct — only the post-save landing screen was wrong. — **Done 2026-08-12**, one commit.
  - **Fix is in `ThemeProvider` itself (`packages/ui/src/theme/theme-provider.tsx`), not in the app.** It was uncontrolled by design — `initialAccent`/`initialPreference` only ever seeded a `useState` at mount — so the only way an external source of truth (the Zustand-backed `accent`/`preference` one level up in `AppThemeProvider`) could reach it after mount was a remount. Added two `useEffect`s that resync `accent`/`preference` state whenever the corresponding `initial*` prop changes; setting state to a value it already holds is a no-op re-render, so this doesn't fight a change that originated from the provider's own `setAccent`/`setPreference` and round-tripped back through `onAccentChange`/`onPreferenceChange`. `AppThemeProvider`'s `key={...}` is gone — no other change needed there.
  - **Second, unmentioned instance of the same bug, fixed for free.** `customize-profile-screen.tsx`'s own inline `<ThemeProvider initialPreference={theme.scheme} initialAccent={draft.accent}>` around the live preview card had the identical defect — tapping a different accent swatch never updated the preview, since that `ThemeProvider` wasn't keyed either and 3b.6's closing note never claimed to have checked it. Same root cause, same fix, no code change needed at the call site.
  - **Measured live** against a throwaway `themeremount0812@smoke.gmrlog.local` probe (tokens injected into `localStorage`'s `gmrlog.secure.*`-prefixed keys, 3b.5's method), Metro restarted first per the `CI=1` watcher trap. Dark, 1280×800: selected Plasma on `/profile/customize`, confirmed the live preview's border resolved to `rgb(145, 132, 217)` — `plasma.dark.default` (`#9184D9`) exactly, changing with the draft before any save — then saved; the PATCH body read `"accent":"plasma"` and the tab landed on `aria-selected="true"` for the Profile tab at `window.location.pathname === "/profile"`, not Home. Light, 375×812: switched appearance to Light in Settings first (confirmed live, no crash, backgrounds resolved to `rgb(251, 251, 253)` and accent borders to `rgb(111, 95, 208)` — `plasma.light.default`, `#6F5FD0`), then repeated the save with Cobalt from `/profile/customize`; same result, `/profile` with the Profile tab `aria-selected="true"`, PATCH confirmed on the network log.
