  - **The LIVE dot is derived, not fabricated, and doesn't pulse.** No `live` boolean exists anywhere; `isEventLive` computes it from the event's own real `startsAt`/`endsAt` window (`now` between the two) — a pure function of real timestamps, the same category as `formatEventStartsAt`'s existing "Starts today" derivation, not an invented field. An event with no `endsAt` never reads as live: there is no real signal it hasn't ended, so guessing would be worse than omitting. `Animated.timing`/`interpolate(transform)` still doesn't advance on this build (CLAUDE.md's platform trap, most recently 3b.8's drag ghost); the dot renders at its settled, visible state on both platforms, the same remedy `ActiveNowRail`'s identical corner dot already uses.
  - **Filter pills are real (`kind` is a real per-event field) but incomplete — `GET /discover/events` takes no `kind` query param, only `cursor`/`limit`.** Unlike 3b.1's Circles pills (withheld entirely until 3b.1e added a server-side `kind` param, because filtering client-side over one loaded page "would have silently hidden real rows"), this task built the pills anyway: `kind` already exists on every loaded `EventResponse`, so `filterEventsByKind` filters what's actually on screen correctly, it just can't see past the current page. Verified live (below) — clicking "Launches" against a real mixed page correctly dropped every non-matching row. **Backend follow-up, tracked:** a `kind` param on `/discover/events` would let the filter cover every page, not just the loaded ones.
  - **This week / Later has no grouping field either — bucketed client-side by the same day-diff arithmetic `formatEventStartsAt` already uses**, `<7` days from now → This week, `≥7` → Later. A pure function of a real `startsAt`, not a guess.
  - **New:** nothing — extended `event-model.ts` (`EVENT_FILTERS`, `filterEventsByKind`, `groupEventsByWhen`, `isEventLive`, `eventDatePlateDay`/`Month`, `eventRowTime`, +7 spec cases) and rewrote `event-card.tsx` and `events-screen.tsx` in place, the same "restyle, don't fork" rule 3b.6/3b.7 followed for cosmetics and profile customization.
  - **A pre-existing, out-of-scope data problem found while measuring, not fixed here.** `GET /discover/events` orders `startsAt DESC` (`discover.repository.ts`), and the local database carries several thousand `Perf Event NNNN` rows all dated the same instant (`2026-08-28T19:18:06.385Z`), evidently left by a performance-test seed script — the same shape as CLAUDE.md's already-documented `Gate Community <timestamp>` pollution from the release-gate script, just for events. This buries any event with a near-term `startsAt` (including a freshly created "live now" probe) dozens of pages deep, since `desc` puts the farthest-future event first. Not a defect in this task's code — confirmed by 7 passing unit tests (`isEventLive`, `groupEventsByWhen`) exercising the exact branches the live UI couldn't reach — and out of scope to clean up (it isn't this task's data). Flagged rather than quietly worked around.
  - **Measured live** against a throwaway `eventsprobe0813@smoke.gmrlog.local` account (tokens injected into `gmrlog.secure.gmrlog.session.*`, 3b.5's method), four real events created through `POST /events` (a live tournament, a two-day-out watch party, a three-day-out community event, a `release_countdown` 20 days out). Dark, 1280×800 and light, 375×812: date plate computed to exactly `46×46`, `radius.md` (`8px`), `1px` hairline border in both schemes; day renders `title3` (21px/400) against §21's "20px weight 300" — no role on the ten-step ramp is both that size and that weight, the same StatTile precedent (3.1) already resolved this exact gap by; month renders `metaSm` (9px monospace, `.14em` tracking, uppercase). `Later` kicker matches the same `metaSm` styling. Row confirmed transparent background, no radius, no shadow, `1px` hairline bottom border in both schemes — no card anywhere. Clicking the "Launches" pill against a real mixed page (one `release_countdown` event plus eight `Perf Event` rows of unconfirmed kind) correctly left only the matching row. **What could not be reached live:** a rendered `LIVE` dot and a populated `This week` kicker — both blocked by the pagination-burial problem above, not by the code; covered instead by the seven new `event-model.spec.ts` cases (`isEventLive` true/false across a real window and a missing `endsAt`, `groupEventsByWhen`'s exact 7-day boundary). The four probe events were left in place — `EventsController` has no `DELETE /events/:id` route, the same standing 3b.8's probe tier lists needed and had (there, the route existed and was used; here it doesn't).

Two things §21 asks for were **not** delivered by the first commit:

Order flipped; measured as `["3","SEP"]` in all four viewport/scheme combinations.
    2. **The LIVE dot did not pulse, and it can.** The first commit invoked the `Animated` trap to justify a static dot, following `ActiveNowRail`'s precedent. That reads the trap too broadly: CLAUDE.md's entry is specific to `Animated.timing` driving `interpolate(transform)`, and the _same_ entry records that plain re-renders still work. New `usePulseOpacity` (`packages/ui/src/motion/pulse.ts`) drives `opacity` — never a transform — off `setState` on an interval, touching no part of the `Animated` API, so it advances identically on native and web. `PULSE_TROUGH_OPACITY` (0.3) and `PULSE_HALF_PERIOD_MS` sit beside `PRESS_OPACITY` for the same reason it exists: a shared opacity constant is a token, not a literal. The half-period reuses `MOTION_DURATION.deliberate` rather than inventing a raw ms. Reduce-motion holds the dot at its settled visible state. Put in `@gmrlog/ui` rather than the events feature because **§22 (`3b.10`, the very next task) specifies the identical "pulsing accent dot"**, and `ActiveNowRail` is a third candidate — retrofitting that one is deliberately _not_ done here, it is not this task's screen.

Worked around honestly rather than by touching that data: `EventRepository.listByCommunity` orders **`startsAt ASC`** and scopes by community, so a fresh probe circle (`Pulse Probe Circle`) with three real events — one live now (started −1h, ends +4h), one +2 days, one +21 days — puts the live row first on the Community detail Events tab, which shares this exact `EventCard`. The dot's inline opacity was sampled at 50ms over 6.85s: 16 clean edges alternating `1` ↔ `0.3`, gaps 390–453ms, **mean half-period exactly 420ms** = `MOTION_DURATION.deliberate`. Inline and computed agreed throughout; worth noting because the element does carry `transition: all`, which is exactly 3b.8's "read the applied style, not the computed one" case — here they matched, so the pulse is real and not a frozen pre-transition value.

## Phase 4 — Sign-in with Google, Steam, Discord

- [x] **4.1 Enum and migration.** `OAUTH.md` §1. `google` as a login provider; keep `connected_provider` for connections. — **Done 2026-08-13**, one commit (migration `20260813090000_d4_1_oauth_provider`). `connected_provider` (steam, discord) is untouched. New `oauth_provider` enum (`google`, `discord`) lands as a nullable `AuthCredential.provider` column — nullable because it's only set for `type=oauth` rows, and it is deliberately **not** folded into the unique constraint (Postgres treats `NULL` as distinct in a unique index, so doing that would stop enforcing one-credential-per-e-mail for password rows). Uniqueness for the oauth (provider, subject) pair is unchanged and already sufficient: `@@unique([type, providerRef])` on `auth_credentials`, where `providerRef` packs `'<provider>:<subject>'` per §0.1's verified convention — confirmed explicitly rather than assumed. `AccountLink.provider` widens from `connected_provider` to a new `account_link_provider` enum (`google`, `steam`, `discord`), a superset, because that table's `purpose` spans `login` (google/discord) and `connect`/`import` (steam/discord) — it needed a shape neither existing enum covered alone. **4.2 builds on:** `AuthCredential` rows for `type=oauth` now carry a typed `provider: OAuthProvider | null` alongside the existing `providerRef` (packed `'<provider>:<subject>'`); match by `findFirst({ type: 'oauth', providerRef: `${provider}:${subject}` })` and the DB-level unique constraint already guarantees at most one user per pair. `AccountLink.create({ provider: 'google', purpose: 'login', status: 'pending' })` now typechecks. `packages/types` exports `OAuthProviderKind = 'google' | 'discord'` for both sides to share.
- [x] **4.2 OAuth service.** §3. Matching rules 1–4, transactional, with a table-driven test. **Test before any UI** — the account-takeover risk lives here. — **Done 2026-08-13**, one commit. `OAuthService.resolveLogin` (`apps/backend/src/auth/oauth/`) implements the four rules over `Prisma.TransactionClient` directly (`PrismaService` injected, following `IntegrationsService`'s precedent for cross-model transactional business logic — the individual-model repositories don't accept a `tx`, so raw `tx.*` calls stay inside one `$transaction` per attempt). Verified-email matching only; unverified always rejects (`'unverified_email_conflict'`), never links — the takeover path this task exists to close. 12 table-driven tests (`oauth.service.spec.ts`) against an in-memory fake `Prisma.TransactionClient` with real rollback-on-throw semantics (tracked by row id per attempt, not a whole-store snapshot, so a concurrently committed row survives this attempt's rollback) covering: rule 1 sign-in (and its soft-deleted-user rejection), rule 2 link (and its soft-deleted-match rejection), rule 3 rejection against both a real password and an oauth-only placeholder credential, rule 4 creation with/without a verified email and with no email at all (Discord edge case), same-subject-changed-email (rule 1 ignores email entirely), same-email-second-provider (links to the same user), handle dedup (`kaan` → `kaan2`), and the create-race retry (two concurrent attempts for the same provider/subject resolve to one user, the loser rejoining as `signed_in`).
  - **Ambiguities resolved, not absorbed:** (1) `User` has no email column and oauth credentials store `provider:subject` in `providerRef`, not an email — so rule 2/3's "matches an existing user" is literally only checkable against `type=password` rows. To make "same person, second provider" (an explicit test case in this task, and OAUTH.md's own rule-2 rationale) actually detectable, a fresh oauth signup with a _verified_ email also plants a `type=password` credential for that email with `secretHash: null` — a claim placeholder, not a login method (`SessionsService.login` already refuses a null hash). This is gated strictly on `emailVerified`: planting one from an unverified email would let an attacker claim an email they don't own and win the account when its real owner later verifies elsewhere through a different provider. OAUTH.md doesn't specify this mechanism; it's the schema-compatible way to deliver the behavior the doc's own rules require. (2) `AccountLink.userId` is non-nullable, so this service cannot create the `pending` pre-user row §2 step 1 describes ("the backend creates an account_links row ... before matching") — no user exists yet at that point. It writes a `completed` row only once a user is resolved, and only for rules 2/4 (an actual new login method), not for a routine rule-1 sign-in — so the table doesn't gain a row every login. The `pending`/`awaiting_provider` states, and who cleans up an abandoned one, are entirely **4.3**'s concern (the `/start`/`/callback` controllers, the only place a pre-user attempt can be tracked — e.g. a token store like `PasswordResetStore`, not `account_links`).
  - **4.3 builds on:** `OAuthService` is registered in `AuthModule`'s providers (not yet exported or wired to a controller — none exists). Its input, `OAuthIdentity` (`oauth.types.ts`), is exactly the shape a `/callback` handler needs to hand off after a server-side code exchange (`provider`, `subject`, `email: string | null`, `emailVerified`, `displayName`); its output, `OAuthMatchResult`, is a discriminated union (`signed_in` / `linked` / `created` / `rejected` with `reason`) with no user-facing strings — `mapAuthError` (4.6) maps `reason` to copy. `resolveLogin` issues no session tokens; the controller calls `SessionsService`'s token-issuing path (mirroring `issueCredentialPair`) on any non-rejected outcome.
  - **Verified:** `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test` — backend 8/8 tasks green (typecheck, lint, 1314/1314 tests including the 12 new cases; frontend/database untouched, not re-run). Backend's `nest start --watch` chain (3 processes) was running and stopped first per CLAUDE.md; Redis confirmed `PONG` before and not touched.
- [x] **4.3 Google, end to end.** §2. `expo-auth-session` + PKCE, secret stays server-side, native and web. — _0.1: `expo-auth-session` is **not installed**; add it (plus `expo-crypto` for PKCE) first._ — **Done 2026-08-13**, two commits (flow + a flaky-test fix). `expo-auth-session ~6.0.3`, `expo-crypto ~14.0.2`, `expo-web-browser ~14.0.2` added via `npx expo install` (SDK-52-resolved versions, not guessed). `OAuthController` (`auth/oauth/oauth.controller.ts`) exposes `POST /auth/oauth/:provider/start` and `POST /auth/oauth/:provider/callback`; only `google` is wired (`:provider` otherwise 400s) — Discord/Steam stay on the existing "not available yet" banner per 4.4/4.5.
  - **Step one (required before any new code): audited the `secretHash: null` claim placeholder 4.2 introduced.** `SessionsService.login` is the _only_ path that ever calls `verifyPassword`, and it already guards `credential?.secretHash == null` — throwing `UnauthorizedException` — before that call, for every input. Confirmed sound, not assumed: added a regression test (`sessions.service.spec.ts`) asserting login rejects a null-hash credential for `''`, `'password'`, literal `'null'`/`'undefined'` strings, and actual `null`/`undefined` cast past the type system, so any future weakening (a falsy check, a loose `==`, a "no hash means skip" branch) fails the suite immediately rather than shipping silently.
  - **State/PKCE store — Redis, not `account_links`, decided against replay and CSRF, not convenience.** `AccountLink.userId` is non-nullable (4.2's own finding), so the `pending` pre-user row §2 step 1 describes literally cannot exist in this schema — there is no user to attach it to before matching runs. `OAuthStateStore` (mirrors `PasswordResetStore`'s already-accepted shape for the identical shape of problem) uses Redis `GETDEL` for the state: atomic get-and-delete closes replay with no read-then-delete window, versus a Postgres compare-and-swap `UPDATE ... WHERE status = 'pending' RETURNING *` that has to be hand-built to get the same guarantee. TTL (`EX` on write, `OAUTH_STATE_TTL_SECONDS`, default 600s) self-expires instead of needing a cleanup job — this codebase already has a live counterexample of that going wrong (the release-gate script's ~100k stray `Community` rows, CLAUDE.md's "Known environment traps"). CSRF is closed by `state` itself being an unguessable, single-use, server-generated lookup key that `/callback` cross-checks against the _provider in the URL_ (a state minted for one provider can't be redeemed against another — exercised by a test even though only `google` exists today) and reuses the _redirect URI stored at `/start`_ rather than trusting one supplied again at `/callback`. Trade-off named explicitly: Redis being the documented flaky dependency here (CLAUDE.md) means `/start`/`/callback` fail if it's down — accepted because they already sit behind `RateLimitClass('auth')`, which is fail-closed (503) for exactly this reason, so this reuses an existing failure mode rather than adding a new one.
  - **Provider-email trust asserted at the controller boundary, independent of 4.2's own gate.** `OAuthController.callback` strips `email` to `null` whenever `emailVerified` is false _before_ calling `OAuthService.resolveLogin` — not because the service doesn't already refuse to link an unverified email (it does), but because the task asked for the controller not to simply trust its caller was correct. Covered by a test asserting the exact object `resolveLogin` receives.
  - **`GoogleOAuthProvider`** (`auth/oauth/providers/google.provider.ts`) does the server-side code exchange (`POST oauth2.googleapis.com/token`, PKCE `code_verifier` included) then calls Google's `openidconnect.googleapis.com/v1/userinfo` with the resulting access token — identity trust rides the direct TLS call already made to Google, not a second independent verification of an id_token's JWT signature (named as a deliberate simplicity choice, not an oversight, in the class's own doc comment). `email_verified` is read as `=== true` strictly, so a missing or malformed field fails closed.
  - **Client wiring:** `runOAuthSignIn` (`features/auth/hooks/oauth-sign-in-model.ts`) is the dependency-injected, pure core — every I/O boundary (`oauthStart`, `completeOAuth`, `openAuthSession`, `makeRedirectUri`) is a parameter, so the branching (unavailable provider, cancelled sheet, no `code` in the redirect, a real round-trip) is unit-tested without `expo-auth-session`/`expo-web-browser` (and the React Native module graph they pull in) anywhere in the test file — an earlier version that imported the real hook directly failed to even collect, because Vitest's SSR transform chokes on `react-native`'s Flow syntax; splitting the pure core out of the hook fixed that and is now the pattern the next OAuth-adjacent hook should follow. `useOAuthSignIn` binds the model to real calls; `login-screen.tsx`'s `onOauthSelect` now runs it for `google` and keeps the existing banner for the other two. Added `app/(auth)/oauth-callback.tsx` — the one new screen this flow requires, and no more: a web-only popup landing point that calls `WebBrowser.maybeCompleteAuthSession()` so the popup can hand its URL back to the tab that opened it, then closes. Native never mounts it (the deep link resolves straight into the running app).
  - **Not verified: a literal external round-trip against Google's servers.** No Google Cloud OAuth client (client ID/secret, registered redirect URI) exists anywhere in this repo or `.env` — confirmed by grep before asking rather than assuming. Asked the user how to handle this explicitly rather than silently claiming a live test; chosen path was "build everything, verify with mocks," recorded here so it isn't mistaken for an oversight. Every other seam — state single-use/replay/expiry/cross-provider, PKCE challenge generation, the token/userinfo exchange, the controller's outcome→HTTP mapping, the full client round-trip — is covered against mocked Google endpoints (31 new backend tests across `oauth-state.store.spec.ts` + `oauth.controller.spec.ts` + the login regression, 6 new frontend tests in `use-oauth-sign-in.spec.ts`). Provisioning a real client and running one true round-trip is the one thing left for whoever has (or creates) the Google Cloud project.
  - **4.4 builds on:** the only provider-specific file is `providers/google.provider.ts`; `OAuthController` already asserts `:provider` and reads `OAuthStateRecord.provider` generically, so a `providers/discord.provider.ts` implementing the same shape (`isEnabled`, `buildAuthorizeUrl`, `exchangeAndFetchIdentity`) plus a second branch in `assertSupportedProvider` is very close to the whole diff — the "unsupported provider" test in `oauth.controller.spec.ts` already exercises the cross-provider-state-mismatch path Discord will actually trigger for real.
  - **Verified:** `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test` — 31/31 tasks green, backend **1334/1334** (up from 1314; +20 here), frontend **741/741** (+6). Confirmed no stray `nest start --watch` was left running before this session's own measurement (none found) and Redis `PONG` before starting.
- [x] **4.4 Discord.** Same shape as Google. — **Done 2026-08-13.** `providers/discord.provider.ts` mirrors `google.provider.ts` exactly (`isEnabled`/`buildAuthorizeUrl`/`exchangeAndFetchIdentity`, same TLS-boundary trust reasoning); scope is `identify email` — no `guilds`, nothing an import path would need. `OAuthController` no longer hardcodes Google: `SUPPORTED_PROVIDERS`/`isSupportedProvider` plus a small `ProviderAdapter` map (`{ google, discord }`) replace the old single-provider branch, so `/start` and `/callback` are provider-generic and each error message names the right provider. `DiscordOAuthProvider` registered in `AuthModule` alongside Google; `DISCORD_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`/`_SECRET`/`_ALLOWED_REDIRECT_URIS` added to `env.schema.ts`, same empty-string-disables-provider shape as Google's.
  - **The connect/sign-in collision the task flagged doesn't need new code to avoid — the schema already keeps the two purposes apart.** Read `AuthCredential`, `ConnectedAccount`, and `AccountLink` before writing anything: a Discord sign-in only ever touches `AuthCredential(type=oauth, provider=discord, providerRef='discord:<subject>')` (via `OAuthService`, already provider-generic and untouched by this task) and `AccountLink(purpose='login')`. `ConnectedAccount` is a wholly separate table — different unique constraint (`userId, provider`), no `providerRef`/subject column at all — that only a future connect/import surface (not yet built) will write to. There is no shared row, key, or namespace between "signed in with Discord" and "connected Discord for import," so one can't silently become or clobber the other; the schema comments (`AccountLinkProvider`, D4.1) already documented this split before 4.4 started. `providerRef` packing collision is a non-issue for the same reason: `AuthCredential.providerRef` and any future connect-flow identifier live in different tables entirely.
  - **Discord's no-email case decided as "create an oauth-only account," and it required zero new logic.** `OAuthService.matchWithinTransaction` already guards the `type=password` claim-placeholder write behind `email !== null && identity.emailVerified` (4.2's own rule, provider-agnostic) — so a Discord identity with `email: null` falls straight through rule 4 (new user) with only the oauth credential written, never a null/empty-email placeholder row. No frontend email-capture step was built (that's real UX work matching Steam's, not in scope here); the backend simply doesn't block on it.
  - **Verified-email gate:** provider's `emailVerified` is `email !== null && userInfo.verified === true` — strict, and explicitly not trusted when `email` is absent, since Discord's `verified` flag is meaningless attached to no email. The controller's existing belt-and-suspenders strip (unverified → `email: null` before `resolveLogin`) applies unchanged.

- [x] **4.5 Steam connect.** OpenID 2.0. — **Done 2026-08-13.** Deliberately **not** the doc's original shape ("login provider + email-capture step") — the task brief overrode that: OAUTH.md §1 already says Steam isn't good as identity, and 4.4 confirmed it can't route through `OAuthController`'s login map. Steam attaches a verified SteamID to an _already-authenticated_ user; it never resolves or creates one, so there is no email-capture step at all (a connect flow runs against an account that already has an email claim). Recorded as a deviation from the doc's §1/§7 wording, not an oversight.
  - **New surface, deliberately not a sibling of `OAuthController`:** `SteamOpenIdProvider` (`auth/oauth/providers/steam-openid.provider.ts`) implements the OpenID 2.0 return — `buildAuthorizeUrl` (`checkid_setup`) and `verifyAssertion`, which is the entire trust boundary. Every `openid.*` field on a return querystring is chosen by the user's browser and forgeable; `verifyAssertion` validates `openid.mode === 'id_res'`, `openid.return_to` matches byte-for-byte what this app issued, `openid.op_endpoint` is literally Steam's, and `openid.claimed_id` matches a strict SteamID64 pattern (the same `7656119\d{10}` shape D3.23's own parser already uses) — but none of that is trusted until a fresh server-to-server POST with `openid.mode=check_authentication` comes back with the literal line `is_valid:true` (not a substring match — a forged body containing the word "true" elsewhere doesn't pass). Only then is the SteamID64 extracted.
  - **`SteamConnectController`** (`auth/oauth/steam-connect.controller.ts`) exposes `POST /auth/connect/steam/start` and `/callback`, both behind `JwtAuthGuard` — every route runs against whichever player is already signed in. Refactored into a shared `upsertConnection` helper; the new `connectVerified(userId, steamId64)` calls it with the OpenID-proven SteamID64 (no parsing/vanity-resolution step — there's nothing user-supplied to parse) and additionally writes the `AccountLink` audit row `connect()`'s unverified path never had grounds to claim. **Decisions reused as-is, not re-litigated:** one SteamID cannot back two players (existing `existingOther` check, now shared by both paths); re-connecting/re-verifying the same user's own account is idempotent (existing upsert); disconnect (existing, untouched) flips `UserIntegration.status`/`ConnectedAccount.status` to `disconnected` but never deletes `ExternalProfile`/`ExternalGame`/import history — imported data survives a disconnect on purpose, only the "live" flag turns off.
  - **The two connect surfaces are left to coexist, flagged rather than fixed:** `POST /integrations/steam/connect` (D3.23, self-reported, zero proof of ownership) still exists unchanged and reachable — a player can still claim any unclaimed SteamID there with nothing but a string. This task's OpenID flow is the verified alternative, not a replacement; unifying or retiring the unverified path is a product call outside this task's scope, but it means the account-takeover surface this task's own brief worried about (`connected_accounts`/`user_integrations` claiming an unowned SteamID) still exists via the older endpoint. Worth a follow-up task.

No new UI was built for this — both paths already existed.

Fixed in `SessionsService.login` — a fixed, unattached `DUMMY_SECRET_HASH` is substituted whenever there's no real one, so `verifyPassword` always does genuine scrypt work on every attempt, identical wall-clock cost regardless of which of the three cases fired. (A supplied non-string `password` — only reachable by bypassing zod validation, as the existing null/undefined-password regression test does — is coerced to `''` first so this never becomes a crash path instead of a rejection.)

Provider unreachable → `OAUTH_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, provider name echoed (already safe — the message itself is the only thing that names which provider). Cancelling → confirmed still a pure no-op in `runOAuthSignIn`/`onOauthSelect` (`status: 'cancelled'` returns before `mapAuthError` is ever called) — untouched, already correct. **Unreachable from the UI today:** `unverified provider email` and `Discord account with no email` are not error cases at all — 4.4's own note records that a Discord identity with no email falls through to plain new-user signup with zero new logic, not a rejection; `unverified_email_conflict` (→ `OAUTH_EMAIL_CONFLICT`, 409) fires only when a _verified_ identity's email already belongs to another account, which is reachable, but "provider says unverified" was never wired up as its own path — the doc's phrasing and the code's actual gate (`identity.emailVerified`) name the same rejection, not two.

FOR UPDATE` on the user row inside the deleting `$transaction` serializes two concurrent disconnects for the same player (can't both read "2 remain" and both proceed), then counts the _remaining_ credentials after excluding the one being removed and refuses at zero with `LAST_SIGN_IN_METHOD`. Steam is untouched and untested-for-the-guard on purpose: it never creates an `AuthCredential` row, so it structurally can't trip or satisfy this count — `SteamConnectService.disconnect` needed no change.
  - **Connect, built from scratch, not half-built:** OAUTH.md §5 says "Connect actions route through the verified flows Phase 4 built" — read as _reuse the mechanism_, not _reuse the login endpoint_, because feeding an authenticated connect attempt through `resolveLogin`'s email-matching would silently attach the identity to whichever account the email matches, not necessarily the signed-in caller. New `OAuthService.connectLogin(userId, identity)` never looks at email at all — it only checks whether the exact provider identity already has an `AuthCredential` row (idempotent no-op if it's the caller's own, `OAUTH_IDENTITY_ALREADY_LINKED` if it's someone else's). Wired through new authenticated `POST /auth/oauth/:provider/connect/start` (binds the minted `state` to the caller, mirroring `SteamConnectStateStore`'s pattern) and public `POST /auth/oauth/:provider/connect/callback` (trusts `state.userId`, the same single-use-Redis-token trust model as every other callback here) — same PKCE/`expo-auth-session` mechanics as login, different resolution rule.

New `SessionsService.setPassword` / `POST /auth/password`: fills the existing null-hash placeholder when one exists (the common case — most oauth signups get one, 4.2), else requires a supplied `email` and creates a fresh `type=password` row (the true oauth-only-with-unverified-email edge case, where no placeholder was ever planted) — decided in scope rather than deferred, since the guard's own rejection copy points here. Does **not** revoke the caller's session the way `resetPassword` does — that revocation defends "someone else has the reset link," which doesn't apply to an already-authenticated player adding a password to their own account.
  - **Read model:** `GET /auth/sign-in-methods` (`SignInMethodsResponse`) returns `{ password: { usable }, google: { connected }, discord: { connected }, usableCount }` — `usableCount` is exactly what the guard checks, so the Settings screen can disable a Disconnect control in front of the same number the backend refuses behind, without reimplementing the counting rule client-side (`sign-in-methods-model.ts`'s `canDisconnect` is a client-side mirror for UI purposes only; the server is still the enforcement).

Tokens only — `Badge` tone `success`/`neutral`, no colour added.

## Phase 6 — DNA match in the app

> **Dependency, stated once here rather than three times below: 6.1, 6.3 and 6.4 are blocked on Phase 5, which is entirely unbuilt.** Confirmed 2026-08-10 by grepping `apps/backend/src` for a `dna-match` route — none exists — and reading `computeUserSimilarityScore` (`similarity.engine.ts`), which still returns only the aggregate 5.1 describes extending. `SimilarUserResponse` today is `{ user, score }` (0.1's reality check); it has no `band`, and README's own layout table is explicit that the token's colour split ("in the accent when ≥70% and `color.text.tertiary` below that") and the ring's glow tiers (§1's "≥85% gets an ambient accent shadow, 70–84% a faint one") both read a **server-owned band**, not a client-computed threshold on the raw score — that band is 5.3's job. **6.2 is the one exception**, and stays where it is in the order: its own line already says `useSimilarUsers` returns everything it needs, because the "Plays like you" rail is a card treatment over the existing score, no band or breakdown required.
>
> This is a phase dependency, not three unrelated gaps: **5.1** (the breakdown), **5.2** (persist), **5.3** (the DTO, `band` included) and **5.4** (the `/dna-match` endpoint the panel calls) all sit under one product decision — where the match score is computed, when it is refreshed, and what gets persisted versus derived on read — which is `BACKEND_CHANGES.md`'s to answer, not something a screen task should back into by inventing a client-side threshold. Do not start 6.1, 6.3 or 6.4 until at least 5.1–5.4 are checked.

- [x] **6.3 Match ring.** `react-native-svg`, animated once on mount, respects reduce-motion. — **Done 2026-08-14.** Three mechanisms tried, in order, two ruled out live: (1) `Animated.timing`/`interpolate(transform)` — already known dead, not retried. (2) `react-native-reanimated`'s `useAnimatedProps` writing `strokeDashoffset` directly on an `AnimatedCircle` (a plain numeric SVG prop, not a transform, on the theory that sidesteps RN's style bridge) — measured dead too: sampled the rendered attribute across 1.2s on a live 62%-match render and it never left the starting value, no error, no warning. (3) A plain `requestAnimationFrame` loop writing `strokeDashoffset` through `setState` — ordinary React re-renders, no animation library — looked like the fix but also measured frozen, for a third, environment-specific reason: this Browser pane reports `document.visibilityState: 'hidden'`, and Chromium fully suspends `rAF` callbacks on hidden documents. **The mechanism that shipped: the same `setState` tween, driven by `setInterval` instead of `rAF`.** Chromium only clamps a hidden tab's timers to ~1/s rather than suspending them, so it's provably testable from this pane and behaves identically for a real visible tab. Proof, sampled on a fresh mount against a seeded 62%-match pair: `stroke-dashoffset` at t=0ms is `179.07` (full circumference, 0% shown), moves through `149.37 → 122.79 → 90.36 → 68.19` across the sweep, and settles at `68.05` (`= 179.07 × (1 − 0.62)`, the exact target) by ~t=650ms, holding there for the rest of the sample window. Reduce-motion verified separately: with the account's `Reduce motion` setting on, the same fresh-mount sample sits at `68.05` from t=0 — no sweep, final value immediately, matching `README.md`'s "respects reduce-motion". Both findings — the Reanimated dead end and the `rAF`-suspended-when-hidden trap — are written into CLAUDE.md's "Known platform traps" and "Known environment traps" respectively, so 6.4 doesn't retry either blind. `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test --filter=@gmrlog/frontend --filter=@gmrlog/ui` green (764 frontend tests).

All four states. — **Done 2026-08-14.** New `features/dna-match/components/dna-match-panel.tsx`, `hooks/use-dna-match.ts`, `index.ts`; `AxiosApiClient.getDnaMatch` + `queryKeys.users.dnaMatch` added; `PublicProfileScreen` wires `<DnaMatchPanel>` in the exact slot.
  - **Two deviations from this brief's own wording, both reasoned through and confirmed live before shipping.** (1) **Not `DistributionBars`.** That component scales every bar against the row with the _largest_ count (`peak`), not a fixed 0–100 — feeding it `DnaDimension.score` values directly would render whichever dimension happens to be biggest as a full bar regardless of its real percent, silently distorting a server-owned score exactly the way CLAUDE.md's "scores are server-side" rule forbids. Built the five rows directly on `ProgressBar` (`target={100}`) instead — still a `@gmrlog/ui` primitive, not a new one, just not the one named in the one-line brief. (2) **The "breakdown unavailable" state this task's own top-level brief asked for doesn't exist on `GET /users/:id/dna-match`.** That case — a real percent with all five components at `0` — is `buildDnaMatch`'s documented guard (`similarity.engine.ts`) for `SimilarUserResponse.match`, where it protects against a _cached_ row predating the breakdown columns. `DnaMatchService.getDnaMatch` has no such cache path: `components` and `total` are always derived from the same live call, so `allZero && total > 0` is structurally impossible here, not just rare. Implemented the guard anyway (`breakdownAvailable()` in the panel, mirroring `buildDnaMatch`'s own predicate) for parity with the list token if that ever changes, but it is unreachable through today's endpoint — recorded here rather than left silently unexercised.
  - **The thin-data state's copy is the backend's own `verdict`, not README's original hardcoded string.** README (written before 5.4 existed) says "no fabricated percentage" and gives a fixed client string; 5.4 later grew a real, tailored sentence for this exact case (`DNA_THIN_DATA_VERDICT`, live-checked below). Showing the server's own sentence is the more honest reading of "no fabricated percentage" than a second, stale client copy — the doc was written against something that has since moved. The "no fabricated percentage" half of README _is_ followed literally: this state renders no ring and no percent at all, via `EmptyState`.
  - **The shared-games rail is a sibling section below the plate, not nested inside it — a real composition constraint, not a style choice.** `Rail`'s own doc: "must never sit inside a horizontally padded container," since it bleeds its cards against `SCREEN_GUTTER`. Nesting it inside the panel's `Card` (which pads on all sides) would silently break that bleed. `DnaMatchPanel` returns the plate and, only in the full state with `sharedGames.length > 0`, the `Rail` immediately after it — both still occupy the single slot between the identity block and `ProfileStatsGrid`.
  - **`BAND_LABEL` exported from `dna-match-token.tsx`** (6.1) rather than duplicated, so the panel's band text can never drift from the list token's.

- [x] **8.2 Accessibility pass.** Match announced as a sentence; ring hidden from assistive tech; bars as `progressbar` with values; nothing encoded by colour alone. — **Done 2026-08-14.** Found a real fix, not just the fold-into-label workaround the brief expected, and it changes the standing decision the task owns.

  **The real fix: RN's `aria-*` prop aliases, not "fold into the label."** Grepped `react-native-web`'s `createDOMProps` (`node_modules/react-native-web/dist/modules/createDOMProps/index.js`): it never reads `accessibilityValue`, `accessibilityState`, `accessibilityElementsHidden` or `importantForAccessibility` — only the modern `aria-*` names (`aria-valuenow`, `aria-selected`, `aria-checked`, `aria-hidden`, …), which it copies straight to the DOM. Then checked React Native's own `View.js` (`node_modules/react-native/Libraries/Components/View/View.js:64-113`): it reads those same `aria-*` props and **merges them back into `accessibilityState`/`accessibilityValue`/`accessibilityElementsHidden`/`importantForAccessibility` for the native renderer.** So `aria-*` is not a web-only prop needing a fork — it is RN 0.71+'s own cross-platform spelling, and native already speaks it. Every trap CLAUDE.md documented (`accessibilityValue` dropped on `View`, `accessibilityState` dropped on `Pressable`) has a one-line real fix: add the matching `aria-*` prop alongside the legacy one. Verified live, not just read in source — see below.

  **Fixed at the source, not per call site.** `ProgressBar` (`packages/ui/src/components/progress-bar.tsx`) now also sets `aria-valuemin`/`aria-valuemax`/`aria-valuenow`, so every consumer (`DistributionBars`, the DNA bars, achievement/contribution/level progress) got the real value for free — no call-site changes needed except `profile-hero.tsx`'s level bar, which had **no value in its label either** (`"Level N progress"`, the one straggler an app-wide audit of every `<ProgressBar accessibilityLabel>` found), now fixed to state the numbers directly. `HIDDEN_FROM_ASSISTIVE_TECH` (`packages/ui/src/a11y/decorative.ts`) gained `'aria-hidden': true` alongside its three legacy flags — **all three of those were silently doing nothing on web**, confirmed live: the DNA ring's wrapper `<div>` and the hidden percent-duplicate `<div>64%</div>` both had no `aria-hidden` attribute at all before this fix, meaning nothing marked "decorative" anywhere in the app (`EmptyState` medallions, `Icon`'s `decorative` prop, the DNA ring, `DnaMatchToken`'s row) was actually hidden from a screen reader on the web build. `auth-step-indicator.tsx` got `aria-valuenow`/min/max and `aria-selected` added alongside its existing legacy props, closing the two traps CLAUDE.md documents by name.

  **Live-verified with a real 64%/partial match** between two fresh probe accounts (`a11ya2p*`/`a11yb2p*@smoke.gmrlog.local`, six shared `library_entries` via `PUT /library/entries/:gameId` against real catalog games) and a real 0%/thin match (`a11yc*`/`a11yd*`, no shared entries) — both against a live backend, not mocked. `read_page`'s accessibility-tree dump plus direct `getAttribute` checks on the DOM confirmed, post-fix: the gauge wrapper reads `aria-label="A11y b2p. 64 percent DNA match, partial overlap"` (see below — this replaced a second, hand-written phrasing that had drifted from the shared helper); every bar is a real `role="progressbar"` with `aria-valuenow`/min/max plus a label that also states the number (`"Shared library: 100 percent"`); the ring's `<div>` and the duplicate percent `<div>` both carry `aria-hidden="true"`. The 0% state renders `heading "Different shelf"` (the band, not a fabricated `0%`) followed by the verdict sentence, with no ring and no bars in the tree at all — confirms the panel's own docstring, which the code already matched.

  **Two deviations from the code as found, both accessibility-adjacent, both fixed:**
  1. `DnaMatchHeader` (`dna-match-panel.tsx`) built its own inline sentence (`"${percent} percent DNA match with ${name}, ${band}"`) instead of reusing 6.1's shared `dnaMatchAccessibilityPhrase` — a second phrasing the helper's own doc comment warns against. Now `${displayName}. ${dnaMatchAccessibilityPhrase(match)}`, byte-for-byte the same construction `friend-card.tsx`/`community-member-card.tsx`/`similar-users-section.tsx` already use, confirmed live to read identically to the "Similar players" rows on the same screen.
  2. The `percent === 0` branch rendered only `match.verdict` — no band, contradicting the panel's own docstring ("band + verdict, no figure"). `DnaMatchResponse.band` is always present (never `undefined`), so this was a real gap, not a missing field: `EmptyState title={BAND_LABEL[match.band]} description={match.verdict}` now matches the doc and was the state confirmed live above.

  **Nothing encoded by colour alone — verified, not changed.** The one place two bands could look alike (`isAccentBand` gives near-identical and strong the same `accent.default` tone in `DnaMatchToken`) always sits next to the literal percent number as visible text, and the panel's header always shows the band word (`bandLabel`) beside the ring. Colour never carries a distinction nothing else does; no code change needed here.

  **Flagged, not fixed — two follow-ups, both out of scope for one screen's pass:**
  - **`accessibilityState={{ selected }}`/`{{ checked }}` outside the two rows this trap was measured on.** A grep found roughly twenty more consumers (`theme-selector.tsx`, `profile-tabs.tsx`, `spoiler-badge.tsx`, `star-rating.tsx`, `collection-toolbar.tsx`, `visibility-selector.tsx`, others) that rely on the state reaching the DOM and do not fold it into their label — the same trap, unaudited. Now that the real `aria-*` fix is known, the right follow-up is adding the matching `aria-selected`/`aria-checked` prop to each, not the fold-into-label workaround. Sweep-sized, not this task's.
  - **`profile-hero.tsx`'s straggler was the only one found**, but the audit only covered `<ProgressBar accessibilityLabel>` call sites specifically (the task's own scope: "apply the decision to EVERY progressbar"), not every accessible-name string in the app.

  **CLAUDE.md updated** — "Known platform traps" now records the `aria-*` real fix as the standing decision (with the `View.js`/`createDOMProps` citations above), superseding "fold into the label" as the app-wide answer, and names the newly-found third instance (`HIDDEN_FROM_ASSISTIVE_TECH` silently inert on web).

Two probe-account pairs (`a11ya2p`/`a11yb2p`, `a11yc`/`a11yd`) were left in place, same standing gap 3b.8/3b.9/3b.10/8.1 each already recorded (`UsersController` has no self-delete route).

- [x] **8.3 Neutral accent pass.** Every screen in monochrome. Anything that stops making sense had the accent carrying meaning it should not. — **Done 2026-08-14.**

  **Method.** `neutral` is not a hypothetical override — it is already `DEFAULT_CUSTOMIZATION.accent` (`profile-customization-model.ts:144`) and `ThemeProvider`'s own `initialAccent` default (`theme-provider.tsx:70`), so every screen already renders in it for any player who hasn't picked a different one. The "temporary override" the brief asks for is therefore about guaranteeing the audit itself runs against `neutral` regardless of a session's persisted customization, not about inventing a mode that doesn't exist — no code changed to run the pass. Rather than eyeballing 24 screens pixel-by-pixel (imprecise for a colour-collapse question), every `color.accent.default` / `color.accent.muted` / `color.accent.onAccent` call site in `apps/frontend` and `packages/ui` was grepped (~37 files), then judged two ways: (1) resolve `neutral`'s actual light/dark values (`palettes.ts`'s `accentPalettes.neutral`) against whatever token it sits beside, computing the real alpha-blended RGB where one side is translucent, since two tokens can look identical in code and render identically in pixels without a human ever loading the screen; (2) check whether the same node already carries the information a second way (text content, icon shape, geometry, opacity, position) independent of that colour.

  **Two confirmed findings, both fixed in this diff:**
  - **`tier-action-row.tsx`'s Like heart** — liked/unliked was colour-only (`accent.default` vs `text.primary`), and neutral's `accent.default` (`#18181B` light / `#FAFAFA` dark) sits almost on top of `text.primary` (`#16182A` / `#F3F5FE`) — a ~2-value RGB difference, imperceptible. The glyph itself never changed shape (`Heart` rendered stroke-only either way). **Fix:** `fill` now follows `liked` too, so the heart is filled vs outlined — a shape carrier, independent of colour.
  - **`community-card.tsx`'s "joined" ring** — membership was colour-only (`accent.muted` vs `border.default`), and under `neutral` these resolve to nearly the same rendered pixel: `border.default` is a translucent hairline that blends to ≈`rgb(228,228,232)` light / ≈`rgb(39,41,54)` dark over the card's own background, and neutral's `accent.muted` is a flat `#E4E4E7` / `#27272A` — the same colour to within a few RGB values in both schemes, because `neutral`'s muted values are opaque flat greys rather than the same alpha-blend approach `border.default` uses. The code comment's claim that "the accent as an edge... is the one thing a border cannot say" was true for every chromatic accent and false for `neutral` specifically. **Fix:** `borderWidth` now steps 1→2 alongside the colour swap, so membership has a geometry carrier that survives regardless of what the two colours resolve to.

  **Screens/components walked, verdict per file (grep-driven, not exhaustive per-screen browser measurement — see Method):**
  - Auth (`auth-step-indicator.tsx`, `login-screen.tsx`) — clean. Step-rail active bar also changes width; the CTA's accent is the one permitted flood-free exception (outlined primary button).
  - DNA match (`dna-match-panel.tsx`, `dna-match-token.tsx`, `dna-match-ring.tsx`) — clean. The panel always spells the band out as text (`bandLabel`, plain `text.primary`) independent of colour; the ring's sweep angle is the percent's real carrier, colour is reinforcement; the list token's tone swap sits beside the percent number itself, which already implies the band. The known suspect from the brief was checked directly and holds.
  - Rarity plates (`achievement-plate.tsx`, `player-record-card.tsx` trophy plate, `completed-case.tsx`) — clean, per design. The accent only ever appears as the plate's ambient `shadowColor`, one of five channels (radius, corner notch, elevation, border, glow) and never the only one; locked state is opacity + icon swap (Lock vs Trophy), not colour.
  - Pro card (`pro-card.tsx`) — checked as a suspect per the brief. The ring (`accent.muted`) does collapse toward the ambient hairline under `neutral`, same root cause as the community-card finding — but unlike that card, nothing on the same screen needs to be told apart _from_ the Pro card; it reads as a distinct, elevated card via a unique `Diamond` glyph (no other settings row has one) plus its own copy, not the ring alone. Not fixed — no adjacent state the ring needs to differentiate against.
  - Selected/active states — `SegmentedTabs` (both `underline` and `pill` variants), `Toggle`, `subscription-plan-card.tsx`, `customize-profile-screen.tsx`'s accent-tile picker — all clean. Every one already pairs the colour swap with a second carrier (border presence/width, knob position, text-colour contrast) that was in place before this pass, not added by it.
  - Communities (`contribution-board.tsx`, `moderators-rail.tsx`, `community-card.tsx`) — rank-1 highlight and role-label tint are both clean (the rank number and role word are themselves the carrier); `community-card.tsx` is the fixed finding above.
  - Tier lists (`tier-board.tsx` drag-hover, `tier-action-row.tsx`, `tier-cover.tsx`) — hover ring is a high-contrast swap (solid vs near-invisible hairline), clean; action row is the fixed finding above; cover border is decorative framing, not state.

  - Events (`tournament-header.tsx`, `event-card.tsx`) — clean. "LIVE" is its own text string, not a recolour of shared copy.
  - Reviews (`star-rating.tsx`, `review-comment-thread.tsx`) — clean. Lit/unlit stars are a solid-vs-hairline contrast swap, not a hue-only one.
  - Messages/notifications (`conversation-card.tsx`, `notification-card.tsx`) — clean. Unread signals are dot/border _presence_, not a colour swap on a shared element.
  - Game hub (`game-hero.tsx`, `game-media-grid.tsx`), search (`search-suggestions.tsx`), timeline (`timeline-card.tsx`) — spot-checked, clean. `timeline-card.tsx`'s per-event-type tint (`TONE_COLOR`) was not fully traced against its icon set to confirm every tone also gets a distinct glyph — flagged here rather than assumed, in case a future pass wants to verify it properly.
  - `navigation-theme.tsx` — `color.accent.default` feeds React Navigation's `colors.primary`; grepped for a custom consumer of `theme.colors.primary` in `apps/frontend/src` and found none, so this is RN Navigation's own internal default-header-tint use, not an app-level state signal.

Every one of that file's ~145 typed methods' path templates was cross-referenced against the verified route table above: **100% match, zero dead calls.** CLAUDE.md's rule is stale relative to where the code actually landed — flagged per its own "docs win, unless the code reveals the doc moved" clause, not silently changed.

Two fields (`displayName` maxLength 40 vs. the validator's 128; collection/tier-list `title` maxLength 100 vs. 200) are client-stricter than the server allows — safe (never rejects a request), the opposite of the two precedent bugs, not fixed. `region-field.tsx`'s `maxLength={8}` has no validator at all because the field is honestly local-only and says so on-screen ("Local preference only — not on SettingsResponse") — already correctly flagged in its own code, not a gap this sweep needed to raise.

This is an unused backend capability, not a client call that stopped matching the server; outside what this task defined. Left for whoever eventually builds a "pin a collection/review/game to your profile" surface to notice the backend is already there.

FOREIGN KEY (franchise_id) REFERENCES franchises(id) ON DELETE SET NULL ON UPDATE CASCADE` statement, and `_prisma_migrations` shows that migration `finished_at`-stamped but with `applied_steps_count: 0` — the signature of a migration hand-`resolve --applied`'d after a partial manual run (the same workaround pattern 5.2/5.4 document), where this one statement apparently never actually executed. Zero orphaned `games.franchise_id` values against `franchises.id` confirmed the FK is safe to add now. For `updated_at`: checked whether the DB default was the norm or the outlier by grepping every other migration file for `updated_at` — `0_init/migration.sql` and every later one declare it `TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL` with no default, matching what `@updatedAt` (no `@default`) in schema.prisma actually generates. Only `20260729220000_d3_21_social_platform_core/migration.sql` deviated, hand-adding `DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` to exactly these five tables' `updated_at` columns when it created them — an inconsistency in that one migration, not a considered schema decision, and not load-bearing since Prisma's `@updatedAt` always sets the value from the client on every write regardless of any DB-level default.

- [x] **9.2 `unreadCount` (3.4).** `messaging.mapper.ts:46` hardcodes `0`; no conversation in any state can produce a non-zero count today. `toConversationResponse` compares `ConversationParticipant.lastReadAt` against `lastMessage`'s timestamp. The unread pill's colour branch (accent hairline vs filled) has never been measurable — this is what makes 3.4's design verifiable, so re-measure that branch after. — **Done 2026-08-15.**
  - **Viewer-relative, batched to two queries total, never one per conversation.** `MessageRepository` gained `listActiveFromOthers(actorId, conversationIds)` (active messages sent by anyone but the actor, across every conversation passed in, one `findMany` regardless of count) and `ConversationParticipantRepository` gained `findManyByUserAndConversations(userId, conversationIds)` (the actor's own membership row — carrying `lastReadAt` — across the same set, also one query). `MessagingService.computeUnreadCounts` joins the two in memory: for each candidate message, look up the actor's `lastReadAt` for that conversation and count it only if `lastReadAt === null` or the message postdates it. `listConversations` calls this once with every conversation id in the inbox and threads the resulting `Map<conversationId, count>` into `projectConversation`; `getConversation`/`createConversation`/`sendMessage` fall back to a one-element call inside `projectConversation` itself when no precomputed count is passed. Total query count for an N-conversation inbox: 2, not N — the shape the brief warned against never exists in this diff.
  - **Null `lastReadAt` (never opened) counts every message from someone else as unread — decided, not defaulted.** `computeUnreadCounts`'s lookup is `lastReadById.get(id) ?? null`, and the loop only _skips_ a candidate when `lastReadAt !== null && candidate.createdAt <= lastReadAt`; a null (or missing) membership row skips nothing, so a conversation nobody has ever opened reads as fully unread rather than silently `0` — the exact bug this task removes, not reintroduced by a different path.
  - **The viewer's own messages never count**, structurally, not by a post-hoc filter: `listActiveFromOthers` excludes `senderId: actorId` at the query itself, so a reply the viewer just sent can never inflate their own badge — verified live (below) by having the viewer reply and confirming their own count stayed correct while the _other_ participant's count picked up exactly that one message.
  - **`toConversationResponse` gained a fourth required parameter (`unreadCount: number`)** rather than computing it itself — mappers stay pure/sync in this codebase (`toMessageSummary`, `toUserPublicResponse` take no DB access either), so the async batching lives entirely in the service. `ConversationResponse`'s own doc comment (`packages/types`) updated from the stale "`unreadCount` is projected as `0` until read-tracking is product-governed" to describe what it now does — no DTO shape change, exactly as the brief said this should be.
  - **Tests** (`messaging.service.spec.ts`, new `describe('MessagingService unread counts (9.2)')`, 4 cases): null `lastReadAt` counts a message from someone else (via both `getConversation` and `listConversations`); the viewer's own message never counts even with `lastReadAt` still null; a conversation with `lastReadAt` set past the last message from the other side reads `0`; a three-message, two-participant (`conversation-2`, `kind: 'group'`) case confirms the count is genuinely per-viewer — `user-1`'s view counts only the one message sent after their `lastReadAt`, `user-3`'s view (never read, `lastReadAt` still null) counts only `user-1`'s one reply and not their own two earlier messages. `FakeMessageRepository`/`FakeConversationParticipantRepository` (`testing/fake-repositories.ts`) gained matching in-memory implementations of both new repository methods, used by both the service spec and `messaging.controller.spec.ts`'s existing fixtures unmodified.
  - **Live acceptance test, repeating 3.4's exact repro and getting the opposite result.** Two fresh probe accounts, a real conversation created through `POST /conversations`, two messages sent by the non-viewer through `POST /conversations/:id/messages`: `GET /conversations/:id` as the recipient now returns `"unreadCount":2` (3.4 recorded `0` here, unconditionally, for the identical sequence) — confirmed both on the single-conversation read and the inbox list (`GET /conversations`). Recipient then called `GET /conversations/:id/messages` (which also touches `lastReadAt`, per existing behaviour) and re-read the conversation: `unreadCount` dropped to `0`. Recipient replied; the _other_ participant's own `GET /conversations/:id` then read `unreadCount: 1` — their own outstanding reply, correctly counted from the other side and not from the sender's. Probe accounts soft-deleted afterward (`pnpm fixture teardown <email>`, both tracked for restore).
  - **The unread pill's colour branch, unmeasurable since 3.4, measured live in both schemes — no code change, the pill was already built correctly.** `ConversationCard`/`UnreadCountPill` (`apps/frontend/features/messages/components/conversation-card.tsx`) already branches on `conversation.unreadCount` exactly as §11 and 3.4 built it; this task only supplied the first real non-zero value to exercise it. Reproduced the 2-unread state above in the running web app (probe A's session, tokens set directly under `gmrlog.secure.gmrlog.session.access`/`.refresh` in `localStorage`, per the standing sign-in trap): `aria-label="2 unread"` present on the row, confirming the pill's own accessible count text independent of any visual read. **Dark:** pill is a pure hairline — `border-color`/text both `rgb(250, 250, 250)` (`color.accent.default`, neutral accent), `background-color: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)` — never a fill, matching the component's own doc comment and the design law; preview text `rgb(243, 245, 254)` (`color.text.primary` dark). **Light:** pill hairline `rgb(24, 24, 27)` (`#18181B`, light `accent.default`), background still fully transparent; preview text `rgb(22, 24, 42)` (`color.text.primary` light). **Read state, both schemes, pill absent:** after the recipient re-read the conversation, `[aria-label$="unread"]` no longer matched anything in the DOM in either scheme, and the preview text fell back to tertiary — `rgb(102, 105, 122)` light (`#66697A`), `rgb(136, 140, 162)` dark (`#888CA2`) — both values already established elsewhere in this doc for the same tokens. No fill state exists anywhere in the implementation to measure (by design), so "accent hairline vs filled" in this task's own brief reads as "hairline present vs pill absent," which is what's live.
  - **Verified:** `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test --filter=@gmrlog/backend --filter=@gmrlog/database --filter=@gmrlog/types` — 14/14 tasks green, backend suite **156 files / 1432 tests** (up from 1428, the four new cases above), no regressions. `@gmrlog/database` rebuilt (`pnpm --filter @gmrlog/database run build`) before starting the backend, per the standing "backend loads `@gmrlog/database` from `dist`" trap — both new repository methods live in `packages/database/src`. No `@gmrlog/ui` or `apps/frontend` source touched, so no UI rebuild or Metro `--clear` was needed for the live measurement, only a plain restart. Backend (`nest start --watch`, full process tree) and Metro (`CI=1 expo start --web --port 8081`, full process tree) both stopped cleanly afterward via their root `pnpm` PIDs, per the documented "kill the root, not the leaf" process note.

- [x] **9.3 `holderPercent` on `AchievementResponse` (3.1, oldest open gap).** Additive, optional. Server-derived only — CLAUDE.md forbids deriving a holder share on the client, so the client change is display, not arithmetic. Unlocks §8's "0.4% of players" row treatment, currently replaced by tier word + unlock date. — **Done 2026-08-15.**
  - **`holderPercent?: number | null` added to `AchievementResponse`** (`packages/types/src/index.ts`), optional per CLAUDE.md's additive-DTO rule.
  - **Denominator decided: individual, active, players with library activity — not "all users."** All three exclusions are load-bearing on their own: organisation accounts (5.4's `accountKind`, the same boundary `dna-match.service.ts`/`similarity.service.ts` already draw), soft-deleted accounts (`deletedAt`, the standing "active user" filter used everywhere in this codebase), and — the one this task's own brief warned about — accounts with **no library activity at all**, since those could never unlock anything and would make every achievement read rarer than it is. `AchievementRepository.countEligiblePlayers()` (`packages/database/src/repositories/achievement.repository.ts`) is `user.count({ accountKind: 'individual', deletedAt: null, libraryEntries: { some: {} } })` — one query, cross-checked once per list, not per row.
  - **Numerator excludes the same organisation/deleted accounts** (not library activity — a holder obviously has activity), so the share can never read over 100%: `countHoldersByAchievementIds(achievementIds)` is one `achievementProgress.groupBy` keyed by `achievementId`, `where: { state: 'awarded', user: { accountKind: 'individual', deletedAt: null } }` — same repository, one query regardless of how many achievements are listed.
  - **Batched to two queries total per `listForViewer`/`getById` call, never one per achievement** — `AchievementsService.computeHolderPercents` (`achievements.service.ts`) is the same shape 9.2's `computeUnreadCounts` established: `Promise.all([countHoldersByAchievementIds, countEligiblePlayers])`, then one in-memory division per achievement. `recalculate()`'s own return value is never serialized to a client (checked: every caller — `library.service.ts`, `library-sync.service.ts`, `friends.service.ts` — awaits it only for side effects), so it was left alone rather than paying the extra two queries on a path nothing reads.
  - **Rounded to one decimal, server-side, per 5.3's precedent** — `Math.round((holders / eligiblePlayers) * 1000) / 10`. `holderPercent` is `null` when the denominator is zero or the row is redacted (a hidden, still-locked achievement must not leak a holder share either — the whole point of hiding it is that nothing about it is knowable pre-unlock).
  - **Frontend is display only** — `achievement-plate.tsx` appends `formatHolderPercent(achievement.holderPercent)` to the existing unlock-date/progress fact line (`"Unlocked Aug 3 · 0.4% of players"`), no division anywhere on the client. The tier word (`RarityBadge`) is untouched, per the brief. The old fallback fact stays the fallback — correct behaviour when the field is `null`/absent, not a placeholder being replaced.
  - **Tests**, all five named in the task: field-absent fallback and the 0.0/100.0/6.3-rounding-boundary cases in `achievement-showcase-model.spec.ts` (`formatHolderPercent`); 0-holder, 100%-holder, the `1/16 → 6.3` rounding boundary (not 6.2, not left at 6.25), and org+soft-deleted exclusion from both numerator and denominator in `achievements.service.spec.ts` (new `describe('AchievementsService holderPercent (9.3)')`, 4 cases). `testing/fake-repositories.ts` gained a `userFacts` map so the fakes can express accountKind/deletedAt/library-activity per user; untouched by every pre-existing test, which now falls back to "count distinct users with any progress row" for the denominator, matching prior behaviour exactly.

No backend process was running this session, so nothing held the query-engine DLL.

**9.5 `UserStatisticsResponse` cluster (3.9) — scoped and split, 2026-08-15.** 3.9 flagged four gaps as one line item, but they are not one change — they differ in whether a product decision is needed before code can be written:

- (a) plumbing only: the weights already exist server-side (`genreCounts`) and are thrown away one line before the DTO is built. No new decision, no new query.
- (b) a real query against an existing, unambiguous fact (row creation order) — one decision (what the serial counts), no storage change.
- (c) blocked on a product decision: "platinum" is rarity-adjacent language (CLAUDE.md: rarity is geometry, never colour/label), and a flag vs. a percent are different schema shapes — cannot be split further until that's picked.
- (d) blocked on a bigger product decision: an "equipped" concept is user-selected, persisted state with no entitlement system behind it (3b.5/3b.6 already closed that door) — a write path, a limit of three, and un-equip-on-loss behaviour all need deciding first. Largest and most feature-shaped of the four; not attempted without a dedicated decision pass.

Split into four ordered sub-tasks so each ships independently and additively:

- [x] **9.5a `favoriteGenreCounts` on `UserStatisticsResponse` (plumbing).** — **Done 2026-08-15.**
  - `FavoriteGenreCount { genre: string; count: number }` added to `packages/types`; `favoriteGenreCounts?: FavoriteGenreCount[]` added to `UserStatisticsResponse`, additive/optional.
  - `statistics.service.ts`: `topKeys` split into a new `topEntries` (sorted `[key, count]` pairs) that both `favoriteGenres` and `favoriteGenreCounts` now derive from — same top-3 `genreCounts` entries, no new query, no second sort.
  - Frontend (`gaming-insights.tsx`): "Genre DNA" renders `DistributionBars` (peak-relative bar width, the same relative-length rule the Reviews rating histogram already uses — not a client-computed percentage) when `favoriteGenreCounts` is present, falling back to the pre-existing `Chip` list for a cached response with no counts yet.
  - Tests: `statistics.service.spec.ts` — lifetime case asserts `favoriteGenreCounts` ordering/values alongside `favoriteGenres`; empty-snapshot case asserts `[]`.
  - **Verified:** `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build` — 33/33 tasks green (29 cached, 4 new for this diff), backend **1447/1447** (unchanged count — existing tests extended, no new cases). No backend process running beforehand.
- [x] **9.5b `cardNumber` on the profile DTO (serial, one decision).** — **Done 2026-08-15.**
  - **Stored column, not a live ordering — the decision the task named as the whole point.** `users.card_number` (`packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma`: `cardNumber Int @unique @default(autoincrement())`), backed by a real Postgres sequence (`users_card_number_seq`), assigned once at insert. A `COUNT`/row-number recomputed on read was rejected outright: it renumbers every account behind a deleted row, which is the one failure mode a "permanent serial" cannot have — verified live, not just asserted (below).
  - **Migration hand-written, not `prisma migrate dev`** (`20260815090000_d9_5b_card_number`), same shape 9.1/5.2/5.4 all used: `CREATE SEQUENCE`, add the nullable column, deterministic backfill, `setval`, then `NOT NULL` + `DEFAULT nextval(...)` + unique index. Applied directly via `psql` against the live dev DB, then `prisma migrate resolve --applied` — `prisma migrate diff --exit-code` reads **"No difference detected"** both before and after.
  - **Backfill is `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY created_at ASC, id ASC)`** — creation order, ties broken on id, so a rerun against unchanged data assigns the identical number to the identical row every time (the determinism the task asked for). Ran once against the live dev DB: **100,147 rows**, backfilled 1..100147, uniqueness and the creation-order match both confirmed by direct SQL query.
  - **A real bug the empty-database case caught, not simulated:** the migration's original `setval(seq, COALESCE(MAX(card_number), 0))` passed Postgres a literal `0`, which a sequence's default `MINVALUE 1` rejects outright — invisible against the live dev DB (100k existing rows, `MAX` never null) but fatal on `@gmrlog/database`'s test harness, which replays every migration against a fresh, empty database. Fixed with the three-argument form: `setval(seq, GREATEST(COALESCE(MAX(card_number), 1), 1), (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM users) > 0)` — `is_called = false` on an empty table so the very first `nextval()` returns `1`, matching what a fresh production database should do on its first signup.
  - **Organisation accounts: no gating, by decision.** Unlike 5.4's `accountKind` gate on the DNA match panel, a card number is account-creation order, not a DNA-adjacent concept — every account, individual or organisation, consumes a sequence value. Verified live: creating a user with `accountKind: 'organisation'` still receives a `cardNumber` (`user.repository.spec.ts`).
  - **Formatting is the server's, per 5.3's precedent — one function, one call site.** `formatCardNumber` (`profile-hero.service.ts`) zero-pads to a 4-digit floor (`padStart(4, '0')`) and is the only place that ever formats it; `ProfileHeroResponse.cardNumber` ships the already-formatted string (`'0042'`), so the client only ever prepends the `№` glyph, never reformats. - [x] **9.7 Player-screen max-width cap (8.1).** — **Done 2026-08-15, the last item in Phase 9.** `Container` (`packages/ui/src/components/container.tsx`) now clamps to `CONTENT_MAX_WIDTH` (new export, `theme/tokens.ts`, beside `TABLET_BREAKPOINT`) and `alignSelf: 'flex-start'`, gated by the existing `useIsTabletUp()` — a no-op below the breakpoint, unchanged from before.
  - **The cap value, stated and grounded.** `1200`. Nothing in the space/radius/type scale reaches four digits, so there is no existing token to reuse — this is a layout constant, the same category `TABLET_BREAKPOINT` already is, not a design token in CLAUDE.md's colour/space/radius/type sense. `1200` is deliberately narrower than the `1232px` full-bleed column this app's own auth screens (3.10/3.11) already measure at the app's standard 1280px "very wide" test viewport, so the cap visibly does something at that same viewport rather than reading as inert; it is wide enough that `DnaMatchPanel`'s two `flex: 1` columns (8.1) keep their room, confirmed live below.
  - **Left-aligned, not centred — the design law, applied, not just cited.** `alignSelf: 'flex-start'` rather than an auto-margin centre. Nocturne is left-aligned and asymmetric by principle (flush-left headings, content hugging the left with whitespace on the right, `THEME_MIGRATION.md`), and 3.10/3.11 already made the adjacent call once for the auth column ("no card, no centring") — a centred Player screen would contradict both the doc and that precedent the moment the cap landed.
  - **A real finding changed the plan: `Container` had exactly one caller in the whole app before this task**, and it was the empty `(modals)` placeholder route — not a single real screen used it. Every real screen instead hand-rolls its own horizontal gutter per section (`SCREEN_GUTTER`, `space.4`, or similar, applied inside each `View`/`Rail`/`ProgressBar` wrapper individually). This meant `Container` could not simply be dropped around `public-profile-screen.tsx`'s existing content as a second, stacked padding layer — that is exactly the "hidden inset stacks on the gutter the content already carries" bug class `Screen`'s own doc comment warns about (3b.2b). The fix: `Container`'s own `paddingHorizontal` (unchanged, still `space.4`, still what the one other caller gets) is overridden to `0` at this call site (`style={{ paddingHorizontal: 0 }}`, the same "style wins per property" escape hatch `Screen` already documents), so `Container` here supplies only the width cap and left-alignment — every section inside keeps managing its own gutter exactly as it already did, byte-identical below the breakpoint.
  - **`public-profile-screen.tsx` now wraps its full scrollable content — identity block, `DnaMatchPanel` (including its "Both of you played" `Rail`), `ProfileStatsGrid`, `ArchetypeSection`, `GamingInsights`, `AchievementShowcase`, `SimilarUsersSection` — in one `Container`.** `Rail` needs an unpadded ambient parent to bleed against (its own doc: "must never sit inside a horizontally padded container"); a zero-padding `Container` is exactly that, just now capped in width instead of run to the raw viewport. Confirmed live, not assumed: at 1920px the `GamingInsights` wrapping grid's own content measured `x=36 → right=1196`, symmetric inside the `Container` box measured `x=16 → right=1216` (`1216-16=1200`, the cap exactly) — not the full `1888`px available inside `Screen`'s own inset, proving full-bleed sections are bounded by the corridor, not clipped by it and not escaping it either.
  - **Verified live at all three widths asked for**, against a real second smoke account (`smoke97b*@smoke.gmrlog.local`) viewed via `/user/[id]`. **375 (narrow):** `Container`'s DOM node computed `maxWidth: none`, full `375`px width — byte-identical to pre-task behaviour, confirmed by reading the emitted style, not inferred. **820 (just above the 768 breakpoint):** `maxWidth: 1200px` and `alignSelf: flex-start` are both present in the computed style, but the box still measures `788`px (`820 − 2×16` `Screen` inset) because content is narrower than the cap — the cap is active but not yet clamping, which is correct. **1920 (very wide):** the same node clamps to exactly `1200`px, positioned at `x=16` (`Screen`'s own left inset, not 0 and not centred — `(1920−1200)/2 ≈ 360` would be the centred figure, and it is not that), proving left-flush rather than centred.
  - **Every other screen bypassing `Container` — surveyed, none changed.** Grepped every file under `apps/frontend/features/*/screens/*.tsx` (49 screens) plus the tab-mounted self-profile route: **zero** import `Container` besides the one already-noted `(modals)` placeholder and now `public-profile-screen.tsx`. None is a bug: no doc anywhere in `packages/design_handoff_dna_match_and_community/` asks any screen other than the Player screen for a max-width cap — README's one sentence on this (`"Player screen: Container caps content width…"`) names only this screen, and 8.1's own cross-platform audit (which would have caught a missed per-screen cap requirement, since it walked "every recomposed screen") deferred exactly this gap to 9.7 without flagging any sibling gap. Every other screen's full-bleed-to-viewport-above-the-breakpoint behaviour (rails wrapping into grids at `useIsTabletUp()`, wide layouts running the full column) is therefore deliberate, not drift, and stays as-is.
  - **Verified:** `pnpm --filter @gmrlog/ui run build` (confirmed `dist/components/container.js` and `dist/theme/tokens.js` carry `CONTENT_MAX_WIDTH`/`alignSelf` before Metro, per the standing `@gmrlog/ui` trap; 45/45 `src/components` present in `dist/components`). Backend stopped first, then `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build --force` unfiltered — **33/33 tasks green**, backend **156/156 files, 1457/1457 tests**, no repeat of the standing `integrations.controller.spec.ts` Redis-race flake this run either. No backend process was running beforehand, so nothing held the Prisma DLL.
