## Phase 10 — Production readiness

**Opened 2026-08-16.** Phase 9 closed out the last feature-shaped backend follow-ups; this phase is the gate between "handoff is fully checked" and "this is safe to run in production" — no new features, no new screens. Ten tasks, gated into three groups so later groups aren't started on a shaky earlier one:

- **Gate A — correctness of what already exists:** `10.1` (Redis flake), `10.2` (CI runs the full 33-task graph, not a filtered slice), `10.3` (the web transform defect named in the brief).
- **Gate B — coverage and safety:** `10.4` (E2E critical paths), `10.5` (security pass), `10.6` (performance at real cardinality — the live dev DB already carries ~100k rows per 9.5b's backfill and 3b.7/gate-script note).
- **Gate C — operability and cleanup:** `10.7` (observability), `10.8` (v1 scope removal), `10.10` (visual QA).

**Subscription/billing is explicitly NOT a Phase 10 task**, by product decision recorded this session (carried from `project_subscription_billing_plan`/`project_tasks_md_sequencing` memory) — it stays post-v1, gated behind Phase 10 shipping first, not folded in as a Gate C item.

- [x] **10.1 Redis-race flake root cause.** — **Done 2026-08-16.**
  - **Reproduced, then found the real race — not the connection-outliving-a-test or shared-key shape the brief suggested checking first.** The flake is a genuine ioredis/BullMQ race in `JobsService.getQueue()` (`apps/backend/src/infrastructure/jobs/jobs.service.ts`), not a test-isolation problem: `integrations.controller.spec.ts`'s `beforeAll` boots a real `IntegrationsModule`, whose `IntegrationsWorkerService.onModuleInit()` constructs **five** real BullMQ `Worker`s sharing one real `JOBS_CONNECTION` `ioredis` client (`lazyConnect: true`, so this is the connection's first-ever `connect()`). Each `Worker` brings the shared client up via BullMQ's own `RedisConnection.waitUntilReady` (`node_modules/bullmq/dist/cjs/classes/redis-connection.js:103-116`): check status once, call `client.connect()` only from `'wait'`, otherwise await the `'ready'`/`'error'`/`'end'` events — race-safe by construction. `JobsService.getQueue()` used to duplicate that connect logic by hand: `if (status !== 'ready' && status !== 'connecting') void this.connection.connect()`. That guard **misses ioredis's `'connect'` status** — the real, distinct status ioredis sits in for the instant between the TCP handshake completing and the `'ready'` event firing (`node_modules/ioredis/built/Redis.js:104-120`, `_connect()`'s own check is `status === 'connecting' || status === 'connect' || status === 'ready'`). A `getQueue()` call landing in exactly that window — e.g. `GameMetadataPublisher.enqueueEnrich` (`apps/backend/src/games/metadata/game-metadata.publisher.ts`) firing when integration sync creates a new skeleton game, moments after `IntegrationsWorkerService`'s own Workers kicked off the first real connect — passed the buggy guard and called `connection.connect()` a second time, which ioredis synchronously rejects with `"Redis is already connecting/connected"`. Because the call was `void this.connection.connect()` (fire-and-forget, no `.catch()`), that rejection surfaced as an **unhandled rejection**, matching the reported symptom exactly.
  - **Not any of: connection outliving a test, a shared key between tests, or teardown racing an in-flight command** — checked and ruled out. Each spec file gets its own `Test.createTestingModule` and its own `JOBS_CONNECTION` factory invocation, so there is no cross-file shared `ioredis` instance; `afterAll` closes the app and disconnects cleanly, with nothing in flight at that point in any of the runs below. The race is entirely intra-process, between `IntegrationsWorkerService`'s real BullMQ Workers and `JobsService`'s own redundant connect guard, both racing to bring up the same client.
  - **Reproduction, honestly reported.** The window between ioredis's `'connect'` and `'ready'` statuses on a fast local Redis (docker `gmrlog-redis`, same host) is sub-millisecond, so hitting it by timing luck alone is rare: **150 sequential/parallel runs of `integrations.controller.spec.ts` alone (100 sequential + 50 across two batches of concurrent runs) produced zero failures** — consistent with the Queue check's own note that it "does not fail every time," and with why four separate earlier sessions only saw it intermittently. Rather than keep rolling dice, the exact race was reproduced **deterministically** at the ioredis/BullMQ integration level: a new test (`apps/backend/src/infrastructure/jobs/race-repro.spec.ts`) uses the real `ioredis` client and a real BullMQ `Worker` against the real local Redis (no mocks), hooks the client's own `'connect'` event to call `getQueue()` at exactly that moment instead of relying on OS scheduling, and asserts the outcome. **Against the pre-fix code, this reproduced the exact unhandled rejection (`"Error: Redis is already connecting/connected"`) on the first run, deterministically** — proving the defect directly rather than waiting for timing to cooperate.
  - **Fix: delete the hand-rolled connect guard, don't patch it.** Patching the guard to also check `'connect'` would still leave two independent, uncoordinated pieces of code racing to bring up the same connection — the next ioredis status this codebase doesn't know about would reopen the same class of bug. `new Queue(name, { connection })` already uses the identical `RedisConnection.waitUntilReady` machinery `Worker` does (`node_modules/bullmq/dist/cjs/classes/queue-base.js:23`, same `RedisConnection` class) — so `getQueue()`'s manual connect call was entirely redundant, not merely buggy. `jobs.service.ts` now just constructs the `Queue` and lets BullMQ's own connection bring-up do the one, race-safe thing it already does; the `connect: vi.fn()` value some callers of `JobsService` pass now goes fully unused, which is correct — nothing should call it.
  - **Same race checked for and confirmed real in production, not just tests.** `IntegrationsWorkerService.onModuleInit()` runs identically on every real boot with Redis configured, constructing the same five Workers on the same shared connection; any of this app's other `getQueue()` callers (`SchedulerService`, `FeedFanoutPublisher`, `SearchIndexPublisher`, `EventReminderPublisher`, `uploads.service.ts`, `game-metadata.publisher.ts`) could equally land in that window during a real boot's first few seconds, silently dropping whatever job they were enqueuing (the callers all wrap `getQueue()` in `try`/`catch`, but a `void`'d fire-and-forget rejection from inside `getQueue()` itself was never inside any of those `try` blocks — the exact bug the "papering over" instruction warned against fixing by raising a timeout or adding a retry). Worth carrying forward: this means some fraction of real early-boot job enqueues may have been silently lost to this exact race before today — not verifiable retroactively (nothing logs a `getQueue()`-internal unhandled rejection with enough context to attribute it), but the fix closes the door going forward.
  - **Verified — closing loop counts, per this task's own instruction.** Reproduced the underlying defect deterministically (1/1, forced timing, not chance) before touching any source. After the fix: **`race-repro.spec.ts` (asserting zero unhandled rejections while still forcing the same `'connect'`-window collision) ran 20/20 green**, and **`integrations.controller.spec.ts` + `jobs.service.spec.ts` + `race-repro.spec.ts` together ran 50/50 consecutive green** (real Redis, no mocks, no retries/timeouts/skips added anywhere). Added a fast, deterministic unit case to `jobs.service.spec.ts` pinning the fix at the guard level (`status: 'connect'` ⇒ `connect()` must never be called) so a regression doesn't need a real Redis or a timing window to be caught in CI. Full unfiltered graph: `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build --force` — **33/33 tasks green, backend 157/157 files, 1459/1459 tests** (1457 + 2 new: one `jobs.service.spec.ts` case, one `race-repro.spec.ts` file), no repeat of the flake in this run either. No backend process was running beforehand, so nothing held the Prisma DLL; Redis (`gmrlog-redis`) was found stopped at session start (the standing environment trap) and restarted first.

- [x] **10.2 CI runs the full 33-task graph, not a filtered slice.** — **Done 2026-08-16.**
  - **CI existed (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) but was thinner than it looked.** It ran `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm build` as four separate steps — each a full-workspace turbo invocation for that one task, so no `--filter` undercounting, but also: no Postgres/Redis service containers at all, no `DATABASE_URL`/`REDIS_URL`, no migrations applied, cache-eligible (a stale cached green was possible), no assertion on how many tasks actually ran, and no migration-replay check. A green run here was a weaker claim than it looked, for reasons the filtered-`--filter` framing in this task's own brief didn't quite name.
  - **Confirmed 33 is real, not assumed from 10.1's note.** `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build --dry=json` lists 55 task nodes, not 33 — three packages (`@gmrlog/eslint-config`, `@gmrlog/prettier-config`, `@gmrlog/typescript-config`) are config-only with no scripts, so turbo synthesizes a no-op placeholder per task type for them in the dry graph. A real run only reports tasks that executed a command: verified directly (`pnpm turbo run typecheck --force`, 15 real tasks for one task type across dependencies) that turbo's own `Tasks: N successful, N total` summary line counts real executions only. For the full four-task command that's 8 real packages × {typecheck, lint, test, build} + `@gmrlog/database`'s `generate` (prisma) = 33 — matching 10.1's own reported count exactly, from first principles rather than by trusting the prior note.
  - **Rewrote the workflow rather than patching it.** One job, one command: `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build --force --continue`, `--force` per this task's own instruction (a cached green is a claim about an earlier tree). `--continue` surfaces every failing task in one run instead of stopping at the first, verified against turbo's actual output format by deliberately breaking `@gmrlog/types` locally (`Tasks: 1 successful, 3 total`, nonzero exit) — turbo itself still exits nonzero with `--continue`, so a real failure fails the step before the count assertion is even reached.
  - **Task count is asserted, not eyeballed.** A follow-up step parses the `Tasks: N successful, N total` line and fails if `successful != total` (a partial run that turbo's own exit code already caught) or if `total != 33` (the filtered/misconfigured case this task exists to catch — a narrower run that still exits 0 because everything it did run passed). The 33 is a literal, commented constant, deliberately not derived at runtime — a run that silently computes its own expected count from the current graph could never detect the graph shrinking.
  - **Postgres + Redis as real service containers**, not mocks — 10.1's fix is in ioredis/BullMQ connection-lifecycle code; a mocked Redis is exactly what would not have caught that bug. Ports (5432/6379) and credentials (`gmrlog`/`gmrlog`) match `env.schema.ts`'s own defaults, so no CI-only env fork. Migrations are applied (`pnpm db:migrate:deploy`) before the graph runs, since backend/database tests run against the real schema, not a mock.
  - **Migration check uses the existing `pnpm db:validate:pg` (`packages/database/scripts/validate-postgres.mjs`), not a new `prisma migrate diff --exit-code` step.** That script already does the stronger thing this task asked for: boots a disposable embedded PostgreSQL, runs `prisma migrate dev` against it from completely empty (a real replay of every migration file in order, not a diff against an already-applied dev database), then runs the full persistence suite against the result. This is exactly the shape that would have caught 9.1's migration marked applied whose last statement never ran, and 9.5b's `setval` that only failed on an empty DB — both invisible to a diff against a live database. Self-contained (its own embedded Postgres on a different port), so it doesn't depend on or conflict with the service-container Postgres used by the main graph.

That's a pre-existing local-machine port collision, unrelated to this task's diff, and not reproducible on a clean GitHub Actions runner (the whole reason `services:` containers exist) — but it did block a full local dry-run of `db:migrate:deploy` against the real container from this host, so the workflow's correctness rests on component-level verification (task count, `--continue` failure format, script existence and behavior) rather than one clean end-to-end local run. Worth flagging if a future session hits the same "permission denied to create database" error locally — check `pg_postmaster_start_time()`/`version()` against what `docker exec` sees before assuming the container is broken.

Cause: the workflow's `env:` set both `NODE_ENV: test` and `APP_ENV: test`, but `packages/config/src/index.ts`'s `APP_ENV` schema is `z.enum(['development', 'staging', 'production'])` — no `'test'` member, unlike `NODE_ENV`'s enum which has one. Every spec that boots the real Nest `ConfigModule` (backend) or calls `loadFrontendEnv` (frontend) threw `Invalid backend/frontend environment — APP_ENV: Invalid enum value` before the test body ran; specs whose `afterAll` unconditionally called `app.close()` compounded it into a second `Cannot read properties of undefined` since `app` never got assigned. Never caught locally because no local run (this session's or prior ones') sets `APP_ENV=test` — it was new to this task's own diff, not a pre-existing bug the graph should have already been exercising. **Fix: drop `APP_ENV: test` from the workflow's `env:` block, leave it unset (schema default `'development'`)** — `NODE_ENV: test` already carries the test signal and is the one of the pair actually in its enum; verified locally (`env.schema.spec.ts`, 7/7 green, `NODE_ENV=test` set, `APP_ENV` unset). **Reverified against the real GitHub runner: green.** Run `31912973755`, **4m12s wall-clock, 33/33 tasks** — matches the constant exactly, both the count-assertion step and the migration-replay-from-empty step passed. Full mechanism, code citations, and what it means for the three shipped workarounds (3.2, 3.12, 6.3) are now in `CLAUDE.md`'s "Known platform traps" — RNW's `Animated.timing` and Reanimated's web fallback both tick through `requestAnimationFrame` regardless of `useNativeDriver` (which never actually engages on RNW — `TurboModuleRegistry.get` always returns `null`), and Chromium suspends `rAF` outright on a hidden document, which this pane always reports. Reproduced live this session: a fresh `Animated.timing`+`interpolate(transform)` test route, sampled at rendered `left: 40` twice 9.6s apart (well past its 3000ms duration), never moved, in this pane. Could not get a second sample from a genuinely visible tab — Claude-in-Chrome wasn't connected, and `computer-use`'s browser grant is screenshot-only by design, precisely to block navigating around that restriction — so the finding rests on direct source inspection of the installed `react-native-web`/`react-native-reanimated` packages plus the Page Visibility API's standard, documented behavior, not a second empirical sample. No screen was rewritten; 3.2/3.12/6.3's existing workarounds are correct as shipped and explicitly not reopened. **Gate A is closed.**
- [x] **10.4 E2E critical paths.** — **Done 2026-08-16.** Scoped to five flows (sign up, sign in, log a game, view a profile, send a message) per this session's own brief, not the OAuth/DNA-match/community-join-post-react set this line originally named — narrower and deliberately so, recorded here rather than silently substituted.
  - **Harness: Playwright, driving the real web build in a real Chromium tab.** `apps/frontend/e2e/` — `playwright.config.ts`, `global-setup.ts`, `fixtures/api-client.ts`, five specs under `tests/`. Chosen over a headless-DOM harness because this is an RNW app and the only thing that exercises the real cross-platform render path is a real browser; chosen over reusing the Claude_Browser verification pane because 10.3 root-caused that pane as permanently `document.visibilityState: 'hidden'`, which suspends `requestAnimationFrame` and (per the standing decision on automatic query retry) can silently gate real functional code. A Playwright-launched page is a normal foregrounded tab and does not inherit that class of failure — verified by checking, not assumed: none of the five specs waits on animation completion, only on functional signals (URL, network response, visible text), so the suite would not have silently passed either way, but the harness choice is recorded on its own merits per the brief's own instruction to say why.
  - **Rate limiting: not fought, designed around.** `/sessions` and `/sessions/register` are rate-limited (`RateLimitClass('auth')`, 5 requests / 60s, `apps/backend/src/infrastructure/http/rate-limit.interceptor.ts:43`). `playwright.config.ts` runs all five specs serially (`workers: 1`, `fullyParallel: false`) so nothing races real auth traffic against that limiter. Two fixed, idempotent fixture accounts (`e2e-primary@e2e.gmrlog.local`, `e2e-secondary@e2e.gmrlog.local`) are seeded once by `global-setup.ts`'s `ensureFixtureAccount` (log in if the account exists, register only if it doesn't) and reused on every run — confirmed by running the suite twice back to back locally, both green, no new accounts created the second time. `ensureFixtureAccount` only falls back to register on a `401` from login; every other failure (429 chief among them — hit repeatedly while developing this suite, from the iteration itself, not the design) is rethrown rather than triggering a second, doomed register call against an already-limited route.

Confirmed in the dev DB: four leftover reviews from this session's own debugging iteration all carry a `deleted_at`.

The sign-up spec is the one spec in the suite that must create a brand-new account every run (that's what sign-up means to test), so it leaks exactly one account into the dev DB per run, permanently. Documented in the spec file itself, not hidden. Every other spec was designed specifically to avoid this cost by reusing the two fixed fixture accounts instead of registering fresh ones.
    - **Send-a-message: NOT torn down, checked and confirmed absent, matching the pattern TASKS.md already records for events.** `messaging.controller.ts` has no `@Delete` route on either a message or a conversation. One conversation and one message between the two fixed fixture accounts accumulate permanently, one pair per run.

  - **Two real bugs found by building this, both fixed in this diff, neither a test artifact:**
    1. **`TextField` (`@gmrlog/ui`) had no programmatic link between its visible label and its input at all** — not the three named a11y traps CLAUDE.md already tracks (those are RNW dropping a _supplied_ prop; this is a prop that was never supplied). `label` renders as a plain sibling `<Text>`; the `<TextInput>` got no `accessibilityLabel`, no `aria-labelledby`, nothing — so neither a screen reader nor `getByLabel` had any way to associate "Email" with the actual input, on native or web. Found immediately: the sign-in and sign-up specs could fill nothing until fixed. Fix: `TextField` now defaults `accessibilityLabel` to `label` (a caller-supplied `accessibilityLabel` still wins, so `new-conversation-screen.tsx`'s existing explicit override is unaffected). Rebuilt `@gmrlog/ui`'s dist and reran the full graph — 33/33, no regressions.
    2. **List responses were assumed to unwrap to `{ items: [...] }`; they actually unwrap to a bare array in `data`.** `buildPaginatedEnvelope` (`apps/backend/src/infrastructure/http/envelope.ts:11`) spreads `items` directly into `data`, not into a nested key. Wrong in this suite's own `firstDiscoverableGame` and the message-delivery check on first write, both fixed once the actual envelope shape was read rather than guessed.
  - **Vitest/tsc isolation, checked, not assumed.** No `vitest.config.ts` existed for `apps/frontend` before this task — the default glob would have picked up `e2e/tests/*.spec.ts` and tried to run Playwright's `test`/`expect` under vitest, breaking the existing `test` turbo task. Added `apps/frontend/vitest.config.ts` with `e2e/**` excluded, restating vitest v3's own default exclude array verbatim (an `exclude` override replaces the defaults, it doesn't extend them) so the ~619 existing frontend unit tests are unaffected. `apps/frontend/tsconfig.json`'s `include` list never touched `app/`/`src/`/`lib/`/`features/`/`shared/` only, so `e2e/**` was never in the `typecheck` task's scope either — no change needed there.
  - **CI wiring: a new `e2e` job, deliberately not a turbo task.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gained a second job (`needs: quality`) with its own Postgres/Redis service containers, builds the backend and frontend's full dependency chain (`turbo run build --filter=...@gmrlog/backend --filter=...@gmrlog/frontend`), starts the compiled backend directly (`node dist/main.js`, polled against `/health`) and lets Playwright's own `webServer` config supervise the frontend web build. Not folded into the `quality` job or `EXPECTED_TASK_COUNT=33`: Playwright drives a live server pair, not turbo's cacheable isolated task graph, and folding it in would have meant either inflating that constant for a fundamentally different kind of run or silently exempting E2E from ever failing the build — the count assertion stays honest at 33, unchanged by this diff.
  - **Run on the real GitHub Actions runner, and the first attempt found a real bug in the job's own env block — same shape 10.2 hit.** Run `31915029038`: `quality` passed (3m46s), but `e2e` failed in 48s at `Install dependencies`, before any of its own logic ran. Cause: the job's `env:` set `NODE_ENV: production` (intended only for the backend's own boot), but pnpm itself reads `NODE_ENV` during `install` and skips **all** devDependencies — turbo, Playwright, eslint, even husky's own `prepare` script — when it's `production`. **Fix: no `NODE_ENV` at the job level at all.** Backend boot doesn't set it either, for a second, separate reason found while re-verifying locally: `production` also trips `env.schema.ts`'s `assertProductionRequiredKeys` (D3.20/F6.7's fail-closed check), which needs `JWT_SECRET`/`S3_*`/`SMTP_HOST`/`MEILI_HOST` all explicitly supplied or the server refuses to boot — this job isn't standing up a real production deployment, so it takes the same dev-default secrets and MinIO/Mailpit/Meilisearch placeholders local `pnpm run dev` already relies on. Reverified end to end locally against the exact CI shape before pushing again: built the backend and frontend from the same `turbo run build --filter=...` command the job uses, booted `node dist/main.js` with no `NODE_ENV` set, ran the full suite against the compiled server — 5/5 green.
  - **Verified: full local suite green twice in a row** (`pnpm run test:e2e` from `apps/frontend`, 5/5 both times, ~26–32s), against a real local backend + Postgres + Redis + a real `expo start --web` build, not mocked. Full unfiltered graph reran clean afterward: **33/33 tasks, backend 157/157 files, 1459/1459 tests**, confirming the `TextField` change and new files introduced no regression.
  - **Four more findings from the real GitHub Actions runner, all fixed — local-green was not the whole story, and it took five pushes to close.** Pushing and watching (per this session's own standing practice) surfaced problems a local run against an already-populated, already-online, already-CORS-configured dev machine could never have shown. Told in the order found, including the one dead end, because the dead end is part of the honest record:
    1. **First guess, wrong: the app's own connectivity monitor.** The runner's page showed "You are offline" on every failing spec. `@react-native-community/netinfo`'s web fallback (`nativeModule.web.js`) maps `isConnected` straight to `navigator.onLine`, so the first fix was a test fixture (`e2e/fixtures/base.ts`) forcing `context.setOffline(false)`, then — when that didn't move the needle — an `addInitScript` override of the `navigator.onLine` getter itself. **Neither fixed it.** Both are still in the fixture (harmless, and `setOffline(false)` is the correct default regardless), but they were treating a symptom, not the cause.
    2. **The real cause: `probeApiReachability()` (the connectivity monitor's own real backend ping) was failing for a real reason — CORS.** `env.schema.ts`'s `CORS_ORIGINS` default is only `http://localhost:3000`; the frontend web build serves on `8081`. Without `8081` in the allowlist, the browser's own CORS preflight on every mutating request (`POST /sessions`, `/sessions/register`, `/reviews`, `/conversations`, `.../messages`) blocked the real request from ever reaching the network — which the app correctly read as offline, because from the browser's perspective it was. **Never caught locally** because this machine's own untracked, per-machine `.env` already lists `8081` for this exact reason (present since some earlier session, never named until now). Verified directly before pushing, not guessed: curl'd the actual `OPTIONS` preflight against a locally booted backend, once with `CORS_ORIGINS=http://localhost:3000` (no `access-control-allow-origin` header — reproduces the bug) and once with `8081` added (header present). Fix: `CORS_ORIGINS: http://localhost:8081` in the `e2e` job's env. This took the run from all-five-red to four-of-five-green.
    3. **The fifth spec (sign-up) then hit the auth rate limiter (429) — a budget problem, not a bug in the flow.** `ensureFixtureAccount`'s original order was login-first, register-on-401. CI's Postgres is recreated empty every run, so on CI the account genuinely never exists yet — every run paid a guaranteed 401 before the register that actually succeeds, two requests per account, four total for the two fixture accounts, against a limiter capped at 5 requests/60s (`RateLimitClass('auth')`). Global setup alone was spending 80% of the budget before a single spec ran. Fix: flip to register-first, fall back to login only on `409` (Conflict) — one request per account on CI's actual common case (doesn't exist yet), paying the two-request cost only on the rarer path (a human rerunning locally against a Postgres that already has the account).
    4. **Found while reverifying that flip locally: a fourth, independent bug.** `registerUser`'s idempotency key was deterministic (`e2e-reg-${handle}`, no timestamp) — a rerun against an already-registered handle replayed a long-stale cached success response, tokens included, from a much earlier run whose access token had since expired. Surfaced as a `401` on the very next `/me` call using that "successful" token, immediately after a "successful" register. Fix: timestamped per call, matching the shape `login()`'s key already had.
    - **Local verification of the rate-limit/idempotency fix was itself slowed by the same limiter** — this session's own iteration (repeated local runs, curl preflight checks) had saturated the 5-request/60s window for real; clearing `ratelimit:auth:ip:127.0.0.1` directly in Redis (`docker exec gmrlog-redis redis-cli DEL ...`) was needed to get a clean local read at all. Noted so a future session recognizes the same symptom for what it is rather than re-diagnosing.
  - **Fully green on the real GitHub Actions runner**, run `31918173711`: `quality` in 4m2s, `E2E (critical paths)` in 3m7s, all five specs passing. This — not the local runs — is the verification 10.4 actually needed, since three of the four findings above were things a local pass against this particular machine could never have surfaced.
  - **Verdict per flow:** sign up — pass, real account creation, real redirect, leaks by necessity. Sign in — pass, real login round trip. Log a game — pass, real review created and fully torn down (and now runs against a guaranteed-present fixture game rather than assuming catalog data exists). View a profile — pass, real cross-account profile read. Send a message — pass, delivery confirmed from the _recipient's_ own token, not just sender-side optimistic UI; cannot be torn down, documented.
- [x] **10.5 Security pass.** — **Done 2026-08-16.** Scoped to the two halves this session's own brief named — rate-limit coverage and `:id`-route authorization — not a full dependency-audit/secrets-handling pass; both of those are real gaps still open, recorded at the end rather than silently folded in.

  - **Half 1 — rate-limit coverage, audited route by route, not just confirmed to exist.** `RateLimitInterceptor` (`apps/backend/src/infrastructure/http/rate-limit.interceptor.ts`) resolves a class two ways: an explicit `@RateLimitClass(...)` decorator wins if present; otherwise a path-based fallback (`resolveClass`, `:88-163`) checks, in order, `/search` → `search`, `/uploads` → `upload`, `/follows`|`/reactions`|`/reports` → `social`, `/messages` → `message`, `/sessions`|`/auth` → **`auth`**, else GET/HEAD → `read`, else `write`. That fallback matters here: it means every route under `/auth/*` and `/sessions/*` gets the tightest budget (**5 requests / 60s**, identifier-keyed — `user:<id>` once authenticated, `ip:<addr>` before) even without its own decorator, which is why the table below has fewer real gaps than a decorator-only grep would suggest.

    | Route                                                                                                                                 | Class (source)                                                                                       | Budget                                  | Verdict                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
    | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
    | `POST /sessions` (login)                                                                                                              | `auth` (path fallback — `/sessions` in path)                                                         | 5/60s                                   | Correct, unlimited-login is the classic account-takeover surface.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
    | `POST /sessions/register`                                                                                                             | `auth` (path fallback)                                                                               | 5/60s                                   | Correct.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
    | `POST /sessions/refresh`                                                                                                              | `auth` (path fallback)                                                                               | 5/60s                                   | Correct — a stolen refresh token still can't be hammered.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
    | `DELETE /sessions/current`                                                                                                            | `auth` (path fallback)                                                                               | 5/60s                                   | Tighter than strictly needed (logout isn't sensitive) but harmless.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
    | `POST /sessions/password/forgot`                                                                                                      | `auth`, explicit `@RateLimitClass('auth')`                                                           | 5/60s                                   | Correct — this is the classic email-enumeration/spam vector, explicitly covered.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
    | `POST /sessions/password/reset`                                                                                                       | `auth`, explicit                                                                                     | 5/60s                                   | Correct.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
    | **`POST /auth/password`** (4.7, brief's own first check)                                                                              | `auth`, explicit `@RateLimitClass('auth')` **and** path fallback agree                               | 5/60s                                   | **Already covered — verified, not assumed.** Sets/replaces a password from a placeholder credential; an unlimited version of this route would be a different severity class from an unlimited login, but it isn't unlimited. No fix needed.                                                                                         |
    | **`GET /auth/sign-in-methods`** (brief's second check)                                                                                | No decorator, but path fallback fires (`/auth` in path) before the GET→`read` branch is ever reached | 5/60s                                   | **Already covered, and covered _tighter_ than a typical read** — the path check runs before the method check, so this GET inherits `auth`'s 5/60s rather than `read`'s 300/60s. Also self-scoped only (`playerIdOf(identity)`, no `:id` param) — not an enumeration surface for _other_ accounts regardless of budget (see Half 2). |
    | `POST /auth/oauth/:provider/start` \| `/callback` \| `/connect/start` \| `/connect/callback`, `POST /auth/oauth/:provider/disconnect` | `auth`, explicit on all five                                                                         | 5/60s                                   | Correct, all five (4.4/4.7) carry the decorator directly — confirmed by reading `oauth.controller.ts` route by route, not sampled.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
    | `POST /auth/connect/steam/start` \| `/callback`                                                                                       | `auth`, explicit                                                                                     | 5/60s                                   | Correct.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
    | Every other mutating/list route (`write`/`read`/`search`/`social`/`message`/`upload`)                                                 | path-based default                                                                                   | 180 / 300 / 120 / 60 / 120 / 30 per 60s | Not this task's focus (brief scoped to auth-adjacent coverage) but spot-checked: `resolveClass` runs before the guard chain's identity is known to the controller, so an unauthenticated caller on any of these still gets `ip:`-keyed limiting, never an unlimited pass.                                                           |

    **Finding: no gap.** Every route that creates, resets, or reveals credential/sign-in-method state is inside the 5/60s `auth` budget, and it's not because every route remembered to opt in — the path-based fallback means `/auth/*`/`/sessions/*` can't fall through to an unlimited default even if a future route under those prefixes forgets the decorator. The one place that _could_ still slip through is a future auth-shaped route registered under a **different** path prefix (the fallback keys on the literal string `/auth`/`/sessions`, not on "is this an auth concern") — worth a one-line note for whoever adds the next one, not a fix.

  - **Half 2 — authorization on `:id` reads/writes, enumerated route by route (66 `:id`-shaped routes across 24 controllers), then verified live for the ones whose answer isn't "anyone."** 8.3b's lesson taken literally: a route being gated is not evidence a _second_ path to the same data is gated too, so every service backing a non-public route was read for a **single, shared** ownership/visibility check rather than trusted from the controller's guard alone.

    | Surface                                                                                                               | Who may read/write                                                                                                                                                                                                       | How gated                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Verified                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
    | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
    | Messaging — `GET/POST /conversations/:id`, `GET/POST /conversations/:id/messages`                                     | Participants only                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `MessagingService.requireParticipantConversation` — one private method, called from `getConversation`, `listMessages`, and `sendMessage` alike (no second path found); returns generic `NotFoundException` whether the conversation doesn't exist or the caller isn't in it, so a probe can't distinguish the two. `unreadCount` confirmed viewer-relative (`computeUnreadCounts`, keyed on `actorId`, batched not per-conversation). | **Live**, three fresh accounts: A+B conversation created, C (unrelated) got `404` on `GET /conversations/:id`, `404` on `GET .../messages`, and `404` on `POST .../messages` (send). No 200, no data leak, no existence-leaking 403.                                                                                                                                                       |
    | Collections — `GET/PATCH/DELETE /collections/:id`, `.../follow`, `.../clone`                                          | Visibility-gated read (`assertReadable`, one method, called from every read/follow/clone path — confirmed by grep, not sampled); owner-only write (`assertOwner`)                                                        | Same pattern as tier lists below                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | **Live**: A created a `private` collection, C got `404` on `GET`, `403 FORBIDDEN` on `PATCH`. Correctly distinguishes "can't read" (404) from "can read but can't write" (403) — consistent, not leaking. Cleaned up (`DELETE`, 204) after.                                                                                                                                                |
    | Tier lists — `GET/PATCH/DELETE /tier-lists/:id`                                                                       | Visibility-gated read (`assertReadable`/`canViewerReadTierList`), owner-only write (`assertOwner`, single method, called from both `updateTierList` and `deleteTierList` and from `TierSlotService`'s board-replace too) | Code-read only (same shape as collections, not re-verified live — diminishing return on a third identical pattern)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Not live-verified; pattern identical to collections above.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
    | Reviews / Posts / Comments — `PATCH`/`DELETE :id`                                                                     | Author-only                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `assertAuthor`, one method per service, called from every update/delete site (`posts.service.ts`: 3 call sites, `reviews.service.ts`: 2, `comments.service.ts`: inline equality check in both `updateComment`/`deleteComment`)                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Code-read only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
    | Reactions — `DELETE /reactions/:id`                                                                                   | Actor-only                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `reaction.actorId !== actorId` check in the one `deleteReaction` method                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Code-read only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
    | Communities — member role patch (`PATCH :id/members/:userId`), community patch/delete, wiki upsert, pin create/delete | Role-gated (`canChangeMemberRoles`/`canAdministerCommunity`/`canDeleteCommunity`/`canEditWiki`/`canManagePins`), every mutation calls `requireMembership` first                                                          | Code-read only — this is the privilege-escalation-shaped one (a member changing another member's role), and every mutating method independently calls `requireMembership(communityId, actorId)` then checks the specific role predicate; no route skips it.                                                                                                                                                                           | Code-read only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
    | Integrations — `POST :id/sync`, `DELETE :id`                                                                          | Owner-only                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Both query `prisma.userIntegration.findFirst({ where: { id, userId } })` — ownership is baked into the lookup itself, not a separate check after a plain `findById`, so there's no window where a wrong-owner row could be loaded then "checked."                                                                                                                                                                                     | Code-read only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
    | Library entries — all routes                                                                                          | Always the caller's own library (`playerIdOf(identity)` passed to every service call; `:gameId` identifies a _game_, not another user's row)                                                                             | Structurally can't be an IDOR — there is no `:userId` param anywhere in this controller                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Not applicable.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
    | `/me/pins` (equipped badges, 9.5d)                                                                                    | Self only                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Separate controller (`me-profile-pins.controller.ts`), `@Controller('me/pins')`, no `:id` — the `:id` variant (`GET /users/:id/pins`) is a **different, deliberately public** route (`UserProfilePinsController`, doc comment states the "anyone" answer explicitly)                                                                                                                                                                  | Code-read only; the public route's own doc comment states the intended answer, matching what the code does.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
    | DNA match — `GET /users/:id/dna-match` (5.4)                                                                          | Any signed-in viewer, by design (compares caller vs. subject — that's the feature)                                                                                                                                       | `JwtAuthGuard` only, no ownership check needed since "anyone" is correct here; 8.3b's `buildDnaMatch` gate (`other.accountKind === 'individual'`, `similarity.service.ts:113`) re-checked and confirmed still the single call site — no second path reintroduced since 8.3b                                                                                                                                                           | Code-read only, re-confirmed 8.3b's fix wasn't regressed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
    | Discover `similar-users/:id`                                                                                          | Public/any-viewer, by design                                                                                                                                                                                             | Same `buildDnaMatch` gate as above                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Code-read only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
    | Profile theme — `GET /users/:id/profile-theme`                                                                        | Visibility-gated (`public`/`followers`/`private`), enforced not merely hidden — an unreadable theme 404s rather than omitting one field                                                                                  | `canViewerReadProfileTheme`, checked against real follow-relationship lookup when `visibility: 'followers'`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Code-read only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
    | Statistics — `GET /users/:id/statistics`, profile pins/hero/archetypes/creator/game-hub/`:id` reads generally         | Public, by design (this is a public-profile product, README/CLAUDE.md's own framing)                                                                                                                                     | `OptionalGuestGuard`, consistent across all of them                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Code-read only — consistent with the product's stated design, not a gap.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
    | Events — `POST :id/invite`                                                                                            | **Any signed-in user, not just the organizer or a participant, may invite an arbitrary user to any active event.**                                                                                                       | `EventsService.invite` checks the event and both users are active/exist, but never checks the inviter's own relationship to the event                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Code-read only. **Recorded as a finding, not fixed**: low severity — it creates an invite row + a notification, not a data read/write of someone else's account, so it's an abuse/spam vector (unwanted invite notifications) rather than an IDOR, and closing it needs a product decision (must the inviter be a participant? the organizer? anyone?) this task isn't positioned to make. |

    **No IDOR found.** Every non-public `:id` surface checked resolves through exactly one ownership/participant/role/visibility gate shared by every mutating and reading call site — the 8.3b failure mode (a second, parallel, ungated path to the same data) was the specific thing grepped for at each surface, not assumed absent. The three live checks (messaging × 3 requests, collection read, collection write) all refused correctly, with a 404/403 split that doesn't leak existence. The one real finding (event invites) is scoped and left open rather than fixed blind.

  - **Explicitly not done, flagged rather than silently skipped:** a dependency audit (`pnpm audit` / equivalent) and a secrets-handling review (env var exposure, log scrubbing, JWT secret rotation posture) — the brief's own two-half scope didn't include either, and neither should be assumed clean from this pass.
  - **Live verification used disposable accounts, not the fixture pair.** Reusing 10.4's `e2e-primary`/`e2e-secondary` fixtures would have meant probing IDOR against accounts other sessions also touch; instead this task registered fresh throwaway accounts (`sec-audit-*@smoke.gmrlog.local`, one register call each, well under the 5/60s budget once spread out — hit the limiter once early from testing too fast, which is itself confirmation the limiter is live) against the already-running local backend (port 4000). The one collection created was deleted (`204`) after; the one conversation/message pair and the throwaway accounts themselves are **not** torn down, for the identical, already-documented reason 10.4 recorded — no delete route exists for either. Left in the dev DB alongside 10.4's own leaked fixtures, not hidden.
  - **Full graph reran clean**, backend untouched beyond the audit's own read/probe traffic — no source changes this task, so no rebuild was needed beyond confirming the already-running dev backend (started this session, port 4000) served every probe correctly.

- [ ] **10.6 Performance at real cardinality.** The live dev DB already carries ~100k real rows (9.5b's card-number backfill) plus ~100k orphaned `Gate Community <timestamp>` rows from the release-gate script (CLAUDE.md's own environment-traps note) — a real, if partly artificial, cardinality floor to test list/search endpoints against.
- [ ] **10.7 Observability.** Structured logging exists (`AppLogger`, seen throughout Phase 9's request-completion log lines); what's likely still missing is metrics/tracing/alerting — scope first.
- [ ] **10.8 v1 scope removal.** Strip or gate whatever was built for demonstration/handoff purposes but isn't real v1 scope — candidate list starts with the items in Phase 9's "Deliberately NOT in Phase 9" and "still open" sections (sponsored DTOs' reserved-but-unused enum member, the unlinked pre-3b.8 `TierBuilder` flow) but needs its own audit pass, not assumed from memory.
- [ ] **10.10 Visual QA.** Full-app visual pass across the redesigned screens post-Phase-9, both schemes, at the three widths 9.7 established (375/820/1920) — a broader sweep than any single screen's own closing-note verification.

### 2026-08-15 — 9.5e Badge picker screen, done.

**Carry-over check first, per this task's own brief.** 9.5d reported "31/31 turbo tasks"; 8.4/9.4/9.5b each reported 33. Re-ran `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build --force` unfiltered (no backend process running beforehand, so nothing held the Prisma DLL): **33 total tasks**, confirming 9.5d's 31 undercounted because its own verification command omitted `build` from the task list (`pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test --force`, no `build`), not because the graph itself changed. **32/33 green**; the one failure is `@gmrlog/backend#test`'s `integrations.controller.spec.ts` — the same pre-existing "Redis is already connecting/connected" unhandled-rejection flake 9.5d's own closing note already named and traced to a real ioredis race in `jobs.service.ts`, confirmed unrelated to any diff (reproduces identically in isolation, on unmodified HEAD, before this task changed anything). 9.5d's diff is verified; its five decisions stand as written.

**The picker: a mode on the existing Achievements screen (3.1), reached two ways.** `player-record-card.tsx`'s `onPressBadgeCase` — "tapping opens the badge case" — now pushes `/(app)/achievements?mode=pick` instead of the plain route; `AchievementsScreen` also gained a persistent "Edit badges"/"Done" toggle in its `ScreenTitle` trailing slot, local `useState` seeded from the route param but owned by the toggle afterward (same shape as `customize-profile-screen.tsx`'s widget-reorder mode — a screen-local mode, not a route round-trip on every tap). No second screen; no picker component existed anywhere to reuse for other pin kinds (checked: `game`/`review`/`collection` pins have no picker UI at all yet, only the four API methods) so this is the first, built directly against `achievement-plate.tsx`'s existing rarity plate rather than a new list surface.

**The five things this task had to decide, each explicit:**

1. **Only awarded achievements are selectable, and locked ones are never the only feedback.** `AchievementPlate` gained an optional `picker` prop; when present, the whole row becomes a `Pressable` but stays `disabled` for a non-awarded achievement (its existing `Lock` icon and `LOCKED_OPACITY` dimming already say why — no extra UI needed, and no tap reaches the server to be rejected).
2. **The three-cap is communicated before the fourth tap, not after.** `AchievementsScreen`'s `handleToggle` checks `equippedIds.length >= ACHIEVEMENT_PIN_LIMIT` (a client constant mirroring the server's own, so the picker and the endpoint can never silently disagree about the number) and shows a toast ("remove one first") instead of calling `putMyPin` when full — no network round-trip to discover the limit. Unselected-but-awarded rows at the cap also render visibly receded (`achievement-plate.tsx`'s new `cappedDim`, 0.55 opacity) so the state reads before a tap, not only after.
3. **Order is expressed, not just equipped membership** — a numbered badge (`1`/`2`/`3`, not a checkmark) drawn on the plate itself, the second, colour-independent carrier 8.3's neutral pass requires alongside the border-weight change (1px → 2px, accent) that also marks selection. **Reordering is tap-based** (`ChevronUp`/`ChevronDown` in a new equipped-badge tray above the list), not drag: 6.3 already proved RNW's transform-animation bridge is dead on web, and a control that works on native and freezes on web isn't shippable, so this task didn't re-attempt it. A swap-based pure function, `moveEquippedBadge` (`badge-picker-model.ts`), mirrors `profile-customization-model.ts`'s `moveWidget` exactly — same shape, same no-op-at-either-end behaviour, its own spec (`badge-picker-model.spec.ts`, 6 cases).
4. **Empty is the default, not an error.** Zero equipped renders explanatory copy in the tray ("your three strongest by rarity show automatically... tap a badge below to equip it and take over that choice") rather than an empty state — `selectRecordBadges` (9.5d) already falls back to strongest-by-rarity with no pins, so this is describing real behaviour, not inventing a message for a case that doesn't exist. "Clear — show automatic" un-equips all three in one action, returning to that same fallback.
5. **Organisation accounts: the boundary is real but stayed server-side, by necessity, not by choice.** 9.5d gates equipping on `accountKind === 'individual'` in `requirePinTarget`, both at write and (defensively) at read — but `accountKind` is not exposed to the frontend anywhere in this app; checked by grep across `apps/frontend`, zero hits, it exists only inside `@gmrlog/database`'s Prisma model and never crosses a DTO. Inventing that field now would be backend scope 9.5e's own brief explicitly rules out ("no further backend work — 9.5d's endpoints are already the complete write surface this needs"), so the picker cannot hide itself for an org account client-side. What it does instead: an org account's equip attempt gets the server's own `BadRequestException('Only individual accounts can equip badges')` message, surfaced verbatim through the toast (`pinErrorMessage`, reads `FrontendApiError.message`) rather than a generic failure — the same "relay, don't fabricate" posture the rest of this app takes toward server errors. **Flagged as a real, not-closed gap**: if an org-account picker path needs to be hidden rather than merely rejected, that needs `accountKind` added to a self-facing DTO (`UserSelfResponse` is the obvious candidate) — a small, additive backend task of its own, not invented here.

**Mutations, concretely.** `use-profile-social.ts` gained `useEquipAchievementPin`/`useUnequipAchievementPin` — plain `useMutation` + `onSuccess` invalidation of `queryKeys.pins.me()` (the `useBookmarkPost`/`useCreateQuote` shape in `use-social-actions.ts`), **not** the offline-durable `runOrEnqueueOfflineResult` pattern `usePatchProfileTheme` uses: the picker only renders online (it's showing the live achievement list), so a real failure belongs in `onError`/a caught rejection, not a queued write against data nothing yet confirmed was valid. Reordering issues two `putMyPin` calls in parallel (the moved item and the one it displaced, matching `moveEquippedBadge`'s swap semantics) rather than rewriting every position; `handleClear` issues one `deleteMyPin` per equipped pin. All three handlers guard on a single `pendingId` state to stop overlapping taps from racing each other's position math.

**Not verified in a live browser, by user decision this session.** The environment's standing Redis trap recurred (`gmrlog-redis` had exited; restarted before anything else). The backend then failed to start twice under a wrong invocation — `pnpm start:dev` doesn't exist in `apps/backend/package.json`; the real script is `dev` (`nest start --watch`), confirmed by reading `package.json` directly after two silent ~5-8 minute non-starts. **Worth carrying forward as its own environment note** (see below). Once started correctly the backend came up clean in ~15s. But the Browser pane had no persisted session (onboarding screen, not signed in), and signing in requires typing a password or creating an account — both outside what this agent does regardless of context, per its own standing rules, not a project-specific restriction. Asked the user whether to sign in themselves so live verification could proceed; **they chose to rely on static verification instead** (typecheck/lint/test/build green per the carry-over check above, plus the two new targeted spec files) — the same trade-off 9.5d itself made for its read-only display work, and for the same underlying reason.

**New environment note:** `apps/backend`'s dev server script is **`dev`** (`nest start --watch`), not `start:dev` — the latter doesn't exist and `pnpm start:dev` fails in a way that produced no output and no error in this session (worth a closer look if it recurs), costing two multi-minute silent non-starts before `package.json` was actually read. `pnpm --filter backend run dev` (or `cd apps/backend && pnpm dev`) is correct; `CLAUDE.md`'s own Commands block never actually names a backend dev command, only `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test` and the frontend/Prisma ones — worth adding there so the next session doesn't re-guess it.

**Verified:** `pnpm --filter @gmrlog/ui run build` + `pnpm --filter @gmrlog/types run build` + `pnpm --filter @gmrlog/validators run build` (all consumed from `dist`), then `pnpm --filter frontend run typecheck` and `run lint` clean, then targeted `vitest run features/profile` (127/127, including the two new `badge-picker-model.spec.ts` cases-worth) before the full run. `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test build --force` unfiltered: **32/33 green**, the one failure being the pre-existing Redis flake traced above, confirmed unrelated by reproducing it in isolation before this task's diff existed.

### 2026-08-15 — 9.5d Equipped-badge slot, done. Last feature-shaped task in Phase 9.

**The decision that unlocked this task: equipped badges ARE achievement profile pins, not a second concept.** `TASKS.md`'s own 5.2 note (~L586) had already flagged the shape of the problem: `profile_pin_kind` gained a fourth value, `achievement`, in the D3.29 migration, but `profile-pin.mapper.ts` explicitly threw rather than map it, and `ProfilePinsService.requirePinTarget` had no branch for it — described at the time as "a real bug, not a type-safety formality." That existing machinery (`ProfilePin { userId, kind, objectId, position }`, unique per `(userId, kind, objectId)`, already ordered by `position`) is exactly "equip up to N showcase objects, in your own order" — the same shape the feature brief asked for. Building a second, parallel "equipped badge" table alongside it would have been the drift this effort has spent eight phases avoiding. So this task **completed the pin machinery** rather than inventing a new one: real validation in `requirePinTarget`'s `achievement` branch, and the mapper stopped rejecting the kind.

**The five decisions this task owned, each explicit:**

1. **Limit of three, enforced server-side.** `ACHIEVEMENT_PIN_LIMIT = 3` (`profile-pins.service.ts`), checked in `upsert`: counts the caller's existing `achievement`-kind pins, and rejects a new one at the boundary — _unless_ the `objectId` is already equipped, in which case it's a re-position (a slot-order change), not a fourth pin. Tested at the exact boundary (three succeeds, a fourth fails; re-positioning an already-equipped badge doesn't count against the limit).
2. **Un-earned case: filtered at read, not errored, and the read path is authoritative.** `ProfilePinsService.filterVisiblePins` re-checks `AchievementProgress.state === 'awarded'` on every `listMine`/`listForViewer` call, not just at write time — a stale equip row (the achievement definition could be deleted; nothing cascades from `Achievement` to `ProfilePin`, since `objectId` is an untyped string, not a real FK) simply stops rendering rather than 500ing or needing a cleanup job. Same "omit rather than fabricate" shape `buildDnaMatch` already established for the pre-5.2 all-zero case.
3. **Ordering is stored, not incidental** — but this wasn't a new decision, it's the _existing_ `ProfilePin.position` column (D3.21) and `listByUser`'s `orderBy: [{ position: 'asc' }, ...]`, already exercised by the other three pin kinds. Achievement pins reuse it as-is.
4. **Entitlement: gated on `AchievementProgress.state === 'awarded'`, never on ownership.** There is no ownership/entitlement concept in this app (3b.5/3b.6 closed that door, `BACKEND_CHANGES.md` §8) and this task doesn't invent one — "unlocked" is real, queryable data (`AchievementRepository.findProgress`), which is the only thing `requirePinTarget`'s `achievement` branch checks.
5. **Organisation accounts: excluded, by decision, both at write and at read.** `requirePinTarget` rejects `user.accountKind !== 'individual'` outright (mirrors `AchievementsService.computeHolderPercents`, which already excludes organisation accounts from the holder/eligible-player counts — achievements are already treated as an individual-player concept everywhere else in this codebase, this is that same boundary, not a new one). Read side double-checks the same boundary in `filterVisiblePins` — a legacy or directly-written row for an org account still renders nothing, the same "don't inherit by accident" fix 8.3b made for the DNA-match leak onto organisation profiles. Tested: an org account's `upsert` is rejected, and a row forced into existence anyway (`pins.upsert` directly, bypassing the service) is still filtered out on read.

**Backend, concretely:** `profile-pin.mapper.ts` no longer throws on `kind: 'achievement'`. `profile-pins.service.ts` — `AchievementRepository` injected (new `PROFILE_PIN_ACHIEVEMENT_REPOSITORY` token, wired in `profile-pins.module.ts` via `PrismaAchievementRepository`); `requireActiveUser` now returns the `User` (was `void`) so `requirePinTarget` and the org gate can read `accountKind` without a second query. New `GET /users/{id}/pins` (`user-profile-pins.controller.ts`, `OptionalGuestGuard`, same pattern as `UserAchievementsController`) — the public read side, since a badge equipped for _other people to see_ needs a route that isn't self-scoped; `ProfilePinsService.listForViewer` shares `filterVisiblePins` with `listMine` so the two paths can never disagree about what's visible. `profilePinKindSchema`/`ProfilePinKindValue` widened to include `'achievement'` (additive, per CLAUDE.md's DTO rule — no schema/migration change needed, the enum value already existed in Postgres since D3.29).

**Frontend, concretely — read side and display only, per this task's own scope boundary.** `axios-client.ts` gained `getMyPins`/`getUserPins`/`putMyPin`/`deleteMyPin` (`delete<T>` widened to accept an optional query, additive, existing callers unaffected). `useMePins` (`use-profile-social.ts`) follows the exact `useMeAchievements`/`useMeArchetypes` shape, including the same missing-route degrade. `player-record-model.ts`'s `selectRecordBadges` now takes an optional `pins` argument: when the player has equipped at least one achievement pin, that choice wins outright, in their own `position` order; when they haven't (or the pins route errors and degrades to `[]`), it falls back to the pre-9.5d behaviour — strongest awarded badges by rarity. A pin whose achievement isn't in the awarded set (already filtered server-side, but checked again here — the same defence-in-depth CLAUDE.md's environment traps repeatedly ask for) is silently dropped rather than shown. `selectRarestUnlocks` was changed to always call `selectRecordBadges` with an empty pin list, so "Rarest unlocks" keeps answering "what's objectively rarest" independent of what the player chose to equip — those are different questions and shouldn't collapse into one just because they used to share a selector. `PlayerRecordCard` takes an optional `pins` prop (defaults to `[]`, so every existing untouched call site keeps compiling); wired through both live call sites — `profile-screen.tsx` (`data.pins.items`, via `useProfileScreenData`) and `customize-profile-screen.tsx`'s live preview (`useMePins()` directly, so the customize screen shows your actual equipped badges while you edit other settings, not a mock).

**Not built here, and named as its own task:** a picker UI. No screen calls `putMyPin`/`deleteMyPin` yet — equipping a badge today is only possible via a direct API call. This was this task's own brief's explicit boundary ("if the picker is a screen rather than a slot, that's a second task"), scoped now as **9.5e**.

**Verified:** `pnpm --filter @gmrlog/types run build` + `pnpm --filter @gmrlog/validators run build` (both consumed from `dist`), then targeted `vitest run src/profile-pins` (backend, 16/16 including all six new 9.5d cases) and `vitest run features/profile/hooks/player-record-model.spec.ts` (frontend, 22/22 including four new equipped-pin cases) before the full run. Then `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint test --force` unfiltered — first pass caught a real lint error this task introduced (`@typescript-eslint/prefer-optional-chain` on the `progress == null || progress.state !== 'awarded'` check in `requirePinTarget`, fixed to `progress?.state !== 'awarded'`); clean rerun — **31/31 tasks green**, backend **156 files / 1457 tests**, no regressions. No backend process was running beforehand, so nothing held the Prisma query-engine DLL. Not verified in a live browser: the only reachable UI path is the read/display side (the picker doesn't exist yet, per the scope boundary above), and exercising it live would require either a raw API call against a running backend or waiting for 9.5e — the existing three-badge-slot UI itself is unchanged pixel-for-pixel from its pre-9.5d, already-verified rendering (1.5's sweep), only its data source is new and that's what the unit tests above cover.
